18	## WASB inference API (reverse-engineered 2026-06-06 — for the wrapper)
19	Repo `~/models/WASB-SBDT` (Hydra configs). Usage: `cd src && python3 main.py --config-name=eval
20	dataset=badminton model=wasb detector.model_path=../pretrained_weights/wasb_badminton_best.pth.tar`.
21	Inference core (`src/runners/eval.py::inference_video`): build detector + tracker + dataloader →
22	loop batches `(imgs, hms, trans, ...)` → `detector.run_tensor(imgs, trans)` → per-frame preds →
23	`tracker.refresh()` then `tracker.update(preds)` per frame → `result_dict[img_path] = {x,y,visi,score}`.
24	That dict IS the trajectory we want.
25	- `TracknetV2Detector(cfg)` (`src/detectors/detector.py`): builds model via `build_model(cfg)`,
26	  loads `cfg.detector.model_path` (`checkpoint['model_state_dict']`), DataParallel, eval. Needs
27	  CUDA (asserts GPU). `frames_in`=cfg.model.frames_in, `input_wh`=(model.inp_width, inp_height).
28	  `run_tensor(imgs, affine_mats)` → model → postprocessor → per-frame (x,y,visi,score).
29	  Preprocessing helpers: `dataloaders.build_img_transforms/read_image/get_transform`,
30	  `utils.image.get_affine_transform/affine_transform`.
31	- eval.yaml defaults: model=wasb, detector=tracknetv2, tracker=online, transform=default.

9	
10	Goal: use `sports-data-collector`'s commercially-licensed rally annotations to **train and evaluate a generic temporal rally segmenter** in `badminton-highlight-indexer`, reusable across sports (badminton/tennis/cricket share a `(video_id, ordinal, start_time, end_time)` shape).
11	
12	Key reframing established 2026-05-26: the indexer's `yolo_hybrid` segmenter uses **a stock person detector (person class only) + a velocity heuristic** — there is no trained badminton model. (As of 2026-05-30 that detector is **torchvision Faster R-CNN + supervision ByteTrack**, swapped off AGPL Ultralytics YOLOv8 — PR #8/ADR-005; the velocity-heuristic logic is unchanged.) The collector's data is **temporal-only** (rally `[start,end]`, stroke counts, scores; NO per-frame shuttle `(x,y)`). So this data trains/tunes the **temporal segmentation** stage, NOT a shuttle object detector (that needs coordinate labels → see [[tracknet-wasb-wsl2-decision]] and [[candidate-segments-finetuning]]).
13	
14	Golden-set ground truth for eval (beyond the collector's own annotations) is now planned via **third-party annotation vendors** on licensed/owned video — design + vendor shortlist in [[golden-set-vendoring]].
15	
16	Phased plan (user picked: train a temporal segmenter; exporter lives as a collector CLI subcommand):
17	- **Phase 1 (DONE):** `python -m src.cli.curate export` in sports-data-collector. Emits `<out>/<sport>/<video_id>.csv` (indexer `start,end` format) + `manifest.json`. Filters curated + `is_commercial` by default. Code: `SportsDatabase.get_segment_intervals()` + `SEGMENT_TABLES` map in `src/core/db.py`; `export_eval_set()` in `src/cli/curate.py`.
18	- **Phase 2 (code DONE; bulk re-fetch BLOCKED on cookies):** on-disk videos were **256×144** because the crawler hard-coded `yt-dlp -f worstvideo`. Fixed: `src/core/downloader.py` always fetches best resolution (`bv*+ba/b -S res,...`, merged mp4, ffprobes actual w/h/fps, pins `youtube:player_client=web_safari`, enables EJS solver `--remote-components ejs:github`, auto-adds `~/.deno/bin` to PATH). New `curate fetch` re-downloads existing DB entries at best res, updates `local_path`/`resolution`/`fps` **in place** (rallies + curation preserved — NO clean slate). Guard: below-min-height downloads are discarded, not committed. deno 2.8.0 installed. Both repos now on GitHub under `avidullu` (badminton-highlight-indexer; sports-data-collector created PRIVATE 2026-05-26); both `master` in sync with origin.
19	  - **RESOLVED + COMPLETE (2026-05-26):** root cause of persistent 144p was the `web/web_safari` clients being forced into SABR streaming (strips download URLs); fix = `player_client=default` + authenticated Firefox Premium cookies (`--cookies-from-browser firefox`; Firefox reads cleanly on Windows, Chrome blocked by DPAPI app-bound encryption #10927). Bulk batch initially failed all-at-once from YouTube rate-limiting → added `--sleep`/`--retries`/`--retry-backoff` to fetch. Final result: **all 22 fetchable badminton matches now true 1920×1080** (verified on disk, rallies intact), DB committed. Unfetchable: shuttleset_12 (no URL in ShuttleSet catalog), my_custom_local_match + staging_match_non_comm (mock/non-commercial). ~26GB in data/videos (gitignored). ShuttleSet corpus tops out at 1080p (no 4K). **Phase 2 done.**

9	
10	How we produce trusted **golden-set ground truth** (hand-verified rally `[start,end)` labels) and use it to regression-test the production tool. Full design + vendor shortlist is version-controlled in `docs/GOLDEN_SET_VENDORING.md` (and ADR-006); this file holds the durable decisions.
11	
12	**Keystone insight:** a vendor's label CSV is structurally identical to a pipeline prediction, so the existing `backend/eval/metrics.py` (`interval_iou`→`match_intervals`→`score`) grades all three of: product-vs-GT, vendor-vs-answer-key, and annotator-vs-annotator (IAA). No new scoring code — only a new *source* of intervals.
13	
14	**Decisions locked with the user (2026-05-30):**
15	- **Licensed/owned video only** — vendors never see user uploads (this is the monetization **privacy lever**, see [[monetization-plan]]). Start corpus = the user's own growing match collection + ShuttleSet.
16	- **Rally `[start,end)` depth** (cheapest; extensible later to shot-counts/(x,y)).
17	- **Tiered turnaround:** slow human tier (authoritative) + fast automated tier (CI / "surprise regression"). ⚠️ **Oracle rule:** the fast-tier auto-labeler MUST be a different model lineage than production, else shared blind spots → false green.
18	- **Vendor bake-off**, favouring **Bangalore/India-local** (user is Bangalore-based). *(Superseded 2026-06-02 → started as a single-vendor **Roora** pilot; see Status below.)*
19	
20	**Architecture (partly built — 2026-06-02):** the `AnnotationProvider` registry + `FileProvider` (GS-1), the `regress(video)` runner (GS-2 — `backend/eval/regression.py` + `run_regression.py`), and the `AutomatedProvider` oracle scaffold (GS-4) have **landed**. The vendor-dependent **`HumanVendorProvider` (GS-3)** is the remaining slice, parked until the Roora pilot confirms quality.
21	
22	**Vendor leads (verified India presence + video; pricing NOT verified — all quote-only):** primary = **iMerit** (Bengaluru office, sports domain, enterprise QA) and **Taskmonk** (Bengaluru, platform+service, likely cheaper/agile); heavyweight third = **TELUS/Playment** (Bangalore-founded CV). Excluded: **Karya** (Bengaluru but speech/text only, no video). Tier-2 leads + confidence labels in the doc. (PR #12.)
23	
24	**Status (2026-06-02):**
25	- **Vendor selected for the initial pilot: Roora** (Roora ML Pvt Ltd, HSR Layout, Bengaluru; `info@rooragrp.com`) — site/capability (image/video/text annotation; sports industry) and location verified; **quality TBD via the pilot**. Run as a **single-vendor pilot first**, NOT a multi-vendor bake-off; iMerit/Taskmonk/TELUS are the fallback if it misses the quality bar.
26	- **Annotation guideline authored** (Step 0): collector repo `docs/annotation/` — `ROORA_BRIEF.md` (rally CSV schema: `rally_number,start_time,end_time,shots_count,ending_reason`, mm:ss→seconds), `ANNOTATION_GUIDE.md` (illustrated; three golden rules = ignore dead time / include EVERY rally incl. service faults & lets / shot = one racket-paddle-bat contact; `ending_reason` = forced/unforced-error taxonomy (`winner | forced_error | unforced_error | service_fault | let | other`, shared across net-separated sports; superseded observational `into_net`/`out` 2026-06-06); **singles & doubles** for badminton & pickleball), + intake-manifest & NDA templates.
27	- **Pilot scope = 3 sports** (badminton, pickleball, table tennis), scaling to ~12–15 videos/sport. The **collector now ingests all three** via `import-local` (collector ADR-001 keeps explicit per-sport code over a registry; collector test coverage 87%).
28	- **Still pending:** the internal **gold key** (5–10 expert-labeled clips) and **GS-3 `HumanVendorProvider`**.

===== detector.py run_tensor =====
import os.path as osp
import logging
from collections import defaultdict
from hydra.core.hydra_config import HydraConfig
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image
import cv2
import torch
from torch import nn

from models import build_model
from dataloaders import read_image, get_transform, build_img_transforms
from utils.image import get_affine_transform, affine_transform
from .postprocessor import TracknetV2Postprocessor
from .deepball_postprocessor import DeepBallPostprocessor

log = logging.getLogger(__name__)

class TracknetV2Detector(object):
    
    __postprocessor_factory = {
            'tracknetv2': TracknetV2Postprocessor,
            'deepball': DeepBallPostprocessor,
            }

    def __init__(self, cfg, model=None):
        self._frames_in  = cfg['model']['frames_in']
        self._frames_out = cfg['model']['frames_out']
        self._input_wh   = (cfg['model']['inp_width'], cfg['model']['inp_height'])
        
        self._scales          = cfg['detector']['postprocessor']['scales']

        self._2d_input = True
        model_name = cfg['model']['name']
        if model_name in ['tracknetv2', 'hrnet', 'monotrack', 'restracknetv2', 'deepball', 'ballseg']:
            pass
        else:
            raise ValueError('unknown model_name : {}'.format(model_name))

        _, self._transform = build_img_transforms(cfg)

        self._device = cfg['runner']['device']
        if self._device!='cuda':
            assert 0, 'device=cpu not supported'
        if not torch.cuda.is_available():
            assert 0, 'GPU NOT available'
        self._gpus  = cfg['runner']['gpus']

        if model is None:
            self._model = build_model(cfg)
            model_path = cfg['detector']['model_path']
            if model_path is None:
                output_dir = HydraConfig.get().run.dir
                model_path = osp.join(output_dir, 'best_model.pth.tar')
                log.info('Checkpoint is not specified, so it is set as the best model in {}'.format(output_dir))
                if not osp.exists(model_path):
                    FileNotFoundError('{} not found'.format(model_path))
            checkpoint = torch.load(model_path)
            self._model.load_state_dict(checkpoint['model_state_dict'])
            self._model = self._model.to(self._device)
            self._model = nn.DataParallel(self._model, device_ids=self._gpus)
        else:
            self._model = model

        self._model.eval()

        postprocessor_name = cfg['detector']['postprocessor']['name']
        if not postprocessor_name in self.__postprocessor_factory.keys():
            raise KeyError('invalid dataset: {}'.format(postprocessor_name ))
        self._postprocessor = self.__postprocessor_factory[postprocessor_name](cfg)

    @property
    def frames_in(self):
        return self._frames_in
    
    @property
    def frames_out(self):
        return self._frames_out

    @property
    def input_wh(self):
        return self._input_wh

    def run_tensor(self, imgs, affine_mats):
        imgs  = imgs.to(self._device)
        preds = self._model(imgs)
        pp_results  = self._postprocessor.run(preds, affine_mats)

        results = {}
        hms_vis = {}
        for bid in sorted(pp_results.keys()):
            results[bid] = {}
            hms_vis[bid] = {}
            for eid in sorted(pp_results[bid].keys()):
                results[bid][eid] = []
                hms_vis[bid][eid] = []
                for scale in sorted(pp_results[bid][eid].keys()):
                    scores = pp_results[bid][eid][scale]['scores']

     1	import numpy as np

     3	class Track:
     4	    def __init__(self):
     5	        self._xy_dict    = {}
     6	        self._score_dict = {}
     7	        self._visi_dict  = {}

     9	    def add(self, fid, x, y, visi, score):
    10	        self._xy_dict[fid]    = np.array([x,y])
    11	        self._visi_dict[fid]  = visi
    12	        self._score_dict[fid] = score

    17	            return False
    18	        return self._visi_dict[fid]

    20	    @property
    21	    def last_fid(self):
    22	        fids = list( self._xy_dict.keys() )
    23	        return max(fids)
    24	
    25	    def xy(self, fid):
    26	        if not fid in self._xy_dict.keys():
    27	            raise KeyError('fid {} not found'.format(fid))
    28	        return self._xy_dict[fid]
    29	
    30	    def predict(self, last_fid):
    31	        fid1 = last_fid
    32	        fid2 = fid1 - 1
    33	        fid3 = fid1 - 2
    34	        if self.is_visible(fid1) and self.is_visible(fid2) and self.is_visible(fid3):
    35	            xy1 = self._xy_dict[fid1]
    36	            xy2 = self._xy_dict[fid2]
    37	            xy3 = self._xy_dict[fid3]
    38	            acc     = (xy1-xy2) - (xy2-xy3)
    39	            vel     = (xy1-xy2) + acc
    40	            xy_pred = xy1 + vel + acc / 2
    41	        else:
    42	            xy_pred = None
    43	        return xy_pred
    44	
    45	class OnlineTracker:
    46	    def __init__(self, cfg):
    47	        self._max_disp = cfg['tracker']['max_disp']
    48	        self._fid      = 0
    49	        self._track    = Track()
    50	
    51	    def _select_best(self, frame_dets):
    52	        best_score = - np.Inf
    53	        visi       = False
    54	        x, y       = - np.Inf, - np.Inf
    55	
    56	        xy_pred = None
    57	    
    58	        for det in frame_dets:
    59	            score = det['score']
    60	            if xy_pred is not None:
    61	                qscore  = self._compute_quality(xy_pred, det['xy'], self._track.xy(self._fid-1) )
    62	                score  += qscore
    63	
    64	            if score > best_score:
    65	                best_score = score
    66	                xy         = det['xy']
    67	                x,y        = xy[0], xy[1]
    68	                visi       = True
    69	        return x,y,visi,best_score 
    70	
    71	    def _select_not_too_far(self, frame_dets):
    72	        if (self._fid==0) or (not self._track.is_visible(self._fid-1)):
    73	            return frame_dets
    74	
    75	        frame_dets_ = []
    76	        for det in frame_dets:
    77	            if np.linalg.norm( det['xy'] - self._track.xy(self._fid-1) ) < self._max_disp:
    78	                frame_dets_.append(det)
    79	        return frame_dets_
    80	
    81	    def _compute_quality(self, xy1, xy2, xy3):
    82	        return - np.linalg.norm( xy1-xy2 )
    83	
    84	    def update(self, frame_dets):
    85	        frame_dets     = self._select_not_too_far(frame_dets)
    86	        x,y,visi,score = self._select_best(frame_dets)
    87	        self._track.add(self._fid, x, y, visi, score)

9	
10	def _write_traj(path):
11	    """Synthetic trajectory: two moving segments separated by an invisible gap."""
12	    rows = [("Frame", "Visibility", "X", "Y")]
13	    for i in range(0, 30):          # 0.0-2.9s @10fps: shuttle moving fast
14	        rows.append((i, 1, i * 5, 50))
15	    for i in range(30, 50):         # dead time: invisible
16	        rows.append((i, 0, -1, -1))
17	    for i in range(50, 80):         # 5.0-7.9s: moving again
18	        rows.append((i, 1, (i - 50) * 5, 50))
19	    with open(path, "w", newline="") as f:
20	        csv.writer(f).writerows(rows)
21	    return str(path)
22	
23	
24	def _write_golden(path):
25	    with open(path, "w", newline="", encoding="utf-8") as f:
26	        w = csv.writer(f)
27	        w.writerow(["rally_number", "start_time", "end_time", "shots_count"])
28	        w.writerow([1, 0.0, 3.0, 8])
29	        w.writerow([2, 5.0, 8.0, 6])
30	        w.writerow([3, "", "", 5])          # incomplete -> skipped
31	    return str(path)
32	
33	
34	def test_load_golden_gts_skips_incomplete(tmp_path):
35	    gts = load_golden_gts(_write_golden(tmp_path / "g.csv"))
36	    assert gts == [(0.0, 3.0), (5.0, 8.0)]
37	
38	
39	def test_predict_fn_finds_moving_segments(tmp_path):
40	    pf = make_predict_fn(_write_traj(tmp_path / "t.csv"), fps=10.0, frame_width=100.0)
41	    wins = pf(BASELINE)
42	    assert len(wins) == 2                      # two rallies recovered from the trajectory
43	    assert wins[0][0] < 3.0 and wins[1][0] >= 5.0
44	
45	
46	def test_run_end_to_end_synthetic(tmp_path):
47	    traj = _write_traj(tmp_path / "t.csv")
48	    gold = _write_golden(tmp_path / "g.csv")
49	    res = run(traj, gold, fps=10.0, frame_width=100.0, iou=0.5)
50	    assert res["best_cfg"] is not None
51	    assert res["baseline"]["f1"] == pytest.approx(1.0)   # clean synthetic => baseline already matches
52	    assert "cv" in res and "mean_test" in res["cv"]

### 111. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.945Z)

**Validation complete — and it surfaced an honest, important detail.** Results:

- **(A)** Cache layout correct: 298 window lines, valid manifest.
- **(B) tracker-only re-run** (cache complete, CSV lost): **byte-identical** to the full run. This is the exact reboot scenario that bit you before — now recovers in ~1s instead of an hour.
- **(C) incremental resume** (cache truncated to 150/298, CSV deleted): resumes from 150, completes, and the trajectory matches the full run **except 3 of 300 rows differing by ≤6×10⁻⁵ pixels, with identical visibility**. That residual is *GPU floating-point non-determinism* — the resumed pass re-batches the remaining windows differently, and cuDNN conv/softmax reductions aren't bit-stable across batch boundaries. It is **not** a checkpointing bug (two fresh full runs would differ the same way); the resume logic itself replays the cache exactly. Sub-thousandth-of-a-pixel is utterly negligible for rally-velocity thresholds.
- **(D) cache invalidation**: changing `--limit` (→ different frame count) correctly triggers "cache incompatible → starting fresh."

feat(wasb): resumable detector pass — survive reboots mid-inference

The WASB GPU detector pass (~21k forward passes for a 6-min clip) previously
wrote the trajectory CSV only at the very end, so a reboot mid-pass lost the
entire hour of GPU work (happened during the first full calibration run).

Decouple the expensive, stateless detector pass from the cheap, stateful tracker:

- Detector outputs are cached per sliding-window to <out>_wasbcache/det_raw.jsonl,
  fsync-flushed every --flush-every windows (default 1000), with progress in an
  atomically-written manifest.json. A reboot now loses at most --flush-every
  windows instead of everything.
- On restart we replay the cached windows, skip the DataLoader to the first
  un-cached window, and continue. The accumulation across overlapping windows is
  reconstructed exactly, so the resumed trajectory matches a non-resumed run (to
  within inherent cuDNN float non-determinism).
- The online tracker is stateful (sequential fid) and can't resume mid-stream, so
  it's simply re-run over the full ordered cache — cheap and deterministic. A lost
  trajectory CSV with an intact detector cache now recovers in seconds, not an hour.
- Atomic writes (tmp + os.replace) for the manifest and trajectory; trailing
  half-written cache lines from a crash are detected and truncated on resume.
- Cache auto-invalidates if frames dir / weights / sport / window size / frame
  count change. extract_frames() is now idempotent.

Cache is keyed off the output path and lives next to it (not %TEMP%), so the
Windows runner gets resume for free without changes. 11 new pure-Python unit
tests (no GPU/WSL); validated end-to-end in WSL: tracker-only re-run is
byte-identical, incremental resume matches modulo GPU float noise.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF

## Why
The WASB GPU detector pass (~21k forward passes for a ~6-min clip) wrote the trajectory CSV **only at the very end**, so a reboot mid-pass lost the entire ~hour of GPU work. This actually happened during the first full calibration run (2026-06-06).

## What
Decouple the expensive, *stateless* detector pass from the cheap, *stateful* tracker:

- **Incremental detector cache** — per sliding-window outputs append to `<out>_wasbcache/det_raw.jsonl`, fsync-flushed every `--flush-every` windows (default 1000), with progress in an atomically-written `manifest.json`. Reboot loss is bounded to `<flush-every` windows.
- **Resume** — on restart, replay cached windows, skip the DataLoader to the first un-cached window, continue. The per-frame accumulation across overlapping windows is reconstructed exactly.
- **Tracker decoupling** — the online tracker is stateful (sequential `fid`) and can't resume mid-stream, so it's re-run over the full ordered cache. Cheap + deterministic. **A lost trajectory CSV with an intact detector cache now recovers in seconds, not an hour.**
- **Crash safety** — atomic writes (tmp + `os.replace`) for manifest + trajectory; a trailing half-written cache line is detected and truncated on resume.
- **Auto-invalidation** — cache keyed off output path, invalidated if frames dir / weights / sport / window size / frame count change. `extract_frames()` is now idempotent.

The Windows runner (`wasb_runner.py`) gets resume **for free** — the default cache dir derives from the (stable) `--out` path, not `%TEMP%`. No runner change.

## Tests
- **11 new pure-Python unit tests** (`tests/test_wasb_cache.py`, no GPU/WSL): replay accumulation across windows, resume index, truncated-trailing-line recovery, non-contiguous-window stop, manifest atomicity/compat/invalidation, atomic trajectory write. Full suite **182 green**.
- **End-to-end validated in WSL** on the real frames:
  - tracker-only re-run (cache complete, CSV lost) → **byte-identical** trajectory.
  - incremental resume (cache truncated to 150/298, CSV deleted) → matches the full run except 3/300 rows by ≤6×10⁻⁵ px with identical visibility — *inherent cuDNN float non-determinism from re-batching*, not a checkpointing bug.
  - changing `--limit` (→ different frame count) → correctly invalidates and starts fresh.

1	"""Load hand-annotated ground-truth rally timestamps from CSV.
2	
3	Annotation file format (one file per video):
4	
5	    start,end
6	    00:01:12,00:01:39
7	    102.5,131.0
8	
9	- A header row `start,end` is optional (auto-detected and skipped).
10	- Each timestamp is either decimal seconds (`102.5`) or colon-separated
11	  `MM:SS` / `HH:MM:SS` (`00:01:12`). Both forms may be mixed in one file.
12	- Blank lines and `#` comment lines are ignored.
13	"""
14	
15	import csv
16	import logging
17	from typing import List, Tuple
18	
19	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
20	
21	Interval = Tuple[float, float]
22	
23	
24	def parse_timestamp(value: str) -> float:
25	    """Parse a timestamp string to float seconds.
26	
27	    Accepts decimal seconds or colon-separated MM:SS / HH:MM:SS.
28	    Raises ValueError on anything unparseable so bad annotations fail loudly.
29	    """
30	    value = value.strip()
31	    if not value:
32	        raise ValueError("empty timestamp")
33	    if ":" in value:
34	        parts = value.split(":")
35	        if len(parts) > 3:
36	            raise ValueError(f"too many ':' segments in timestamp '{value}'")
37	        total = 0.0
38	        for part in parts:                 # most-significant first (H, M, S)
39	            total = total * 60 + float(part)
40	        return total
41	    return float(value)
42	
43	
44	def load_annotations(csv_path: str) -> List[Interval]:
45	    """Load and validate ground-truth rally intervals from a CSV file.
46	
47	    Returns intervals sorted by start time. Raises ValueError on malformed rows
48	    (bad timestamp, start >= end) so labeling mistakes surface immediately rather
49	    than silently skewing scores.
50	    """
51	    intervals: List[Interval] = []
52	    with open(csv_path, newline="") as f:
53	        reader = csv.reader(f)
54	        for lineno, row in enumerate(reader, start=1):
55	            if not row:
56	                continue
57	            first = row[0].strip()
58	            if not first or first.startswith("#"):
59	                continue
60	            # Skip an optional header row.
61	            if first.lower() in ("start", "start_time"):
62	                continue
63	            if len(row) < 2:
64	                raise ValueError(f"{csv_path}:{lineno}: expected 'start,end', got {row!r}")
65	            try:
66	                start = parse_timestamp(row[0])
67	                end = parse_timestamp(row[1])
68	            except ValueError as e:
69	                raise ValueError(f"{csv_path}:{lineno}: {e}") from e
70	            if start >= end:
71	                raise ValueError(f"{csv_path}:{lineno}: start ({start}) >= end ({end})")
72	            intervals.append((start, end))
73	
74	    intervals.sort(key=lambda iv: iv[0])
75	    logger.info("Loaded %d ground-truth rallies from %s", len(intervals), csv_path)
76	    return intervals
77	
78	
79	def write_scaffold(csv_path: str) -> None:
80	    """Write an empty annotation file with just the header, for hand-filling."""
81	    with open(csv_path, "w", newline="") as f:
82	        writer = csv.writer(f)
83	        writer.writerow(["start", "end"])

### 133. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.947Z)

[external_agent_tool_result]
1	"""Pure temporal interval-matching metrics for rally evaluation.
2	
3	No I/O, no DB, no global state — every function here is deterministic and unit
4	tested. An "interval" is a `(start, end)` tuple of floats in seconds with
5	start < end. We score a list of *predicted* rally intervals against a list of
6	*ground-truth* intervals via temporal IoU and one-to-one greedy matching.
7	"""
8	
9	from dataclasses import dataclass, field
10	from typing import List, Tuple, Optional
11	
12	Interval = Tuple[float, float]
13	
14	
15	def interval_iou(a: Interval, b: Interval) -> float:
16	    """Temporal intersection-over-union of two intervals.
17	
18	    Returns 0.0 when they don't overlap (or either is degenerate). IoU is
19	    overlap / union, both measured as durations on the timeline.
20	    """
21	    a_start, a_end = a
22	    b_start, b_end = b
23	    if a_end <= a_start or b_end <= b_start:
24	        return 0.0
25	    inter = max(0.0, min(a_end, b_end) - max(a_start, b_start))
26	    if inter <= 0.0:
27	        return 0.0
28	    union = (a_end - a_start) + (b_end - b_start) - inter
29	    return inter / union if union > 0 else 0.0
30	
31	
32	@dataclass
33	class Match:
34	    """One matched predicted↔ground-truth pair."""
35	    pred_index: int
36	    gt_index: int
37	    iou: float
38	
39	
40	@dataclass
41	class MatchResult:
42	    """Outcome of matching predictions to ground truth at a given IoU threshold."""
43	    matches: List[Match] = field(default_factory=list)
44	    unmatched_pred_indices: List[int] = field(default_factory=list)  # false positives
45	    unmatched_gt_indices: List[int] = field(default_factory=list)    # false negatives (misses)
46	
47	
48	def match_intervals(preds: List[Interval], gts: List[Interval],
49	                    iou_threshold: float = 0.5) -> MatchResult:
50	    """Greedy one-to-one matching of predictions to ground truth by descending IoU.
51	
52	    Every candidate (pred, gt) pair with IoU >= threshold is considered; pairs
53	    are claimed highest-IoU first, and each prediction/GT can be used once. This
54	    keeps a single prediction from being credited for two ground-truth rallies
55	    (and vice versa).
56	    """
57	    candidates = []
58	    for pi, p in enumerate(preds):
59	        for gi, g in enumerate(gts):
60	            iou = interval_iou(p, g)
61	            if iou >= iou_threshold:
62	                candidates.append((iou, pi, gi))
63	    # Highest IoU first; ties broken by indices for determinism.
64	    candidates.sort(key=lambda c: (-c[0], c[1], c[2]))
65	
66	    used_pred, used_gt = set(), set()
67	    matches: List[Match] = []
68	    for iou, pi, gi in candidates:
69	        if pi in used_pred or gi in used_gt:
70	            continue
71	        used_pred.add(pi)
72	        used_gt.add(gi)
73	        matches.append(Match(pred_index=pi, gt_index=gi, iou=iou))
74	
75	    unmatched_pred = [i for i in range(len(preds)) if i not in used_pred]
76	    unmatched_gt = [i for i in range(len(gts)) if i not in used_gt]
77	    return MatchResult(matches=matches,
78	                       unmatched_pred_indices=unmatched_pred,
79	                       unmatched_gt_indices=unmatched_gt)
80	
81	
82	@dataclass
83	class Score:
84	    """Aggregate metrics for one (predictions vs ground-truth) evaluation."""
85	    iou_threshold: float
86	    num_pred: int
87	    num_gt: int
88	    true_positives: int
89	    false_positives: int
90	    false_negatives: int
91	    precision: float
92	    recall: float
93	    f1: float
94	    mean_iou: float            # over matched pairs (0.0 if none)
95	    mean_start_error: float    # mean |pred_start - gt_start| over matches, seconds
96	    mean_end_error: float      # mean |pred_end - gt_end| over matches, seconds
97	
98	    def as_dict(self) -> dict:
99	        return self.__dict__.copy()
100	
101	
102	def score(preds: List[Interval], gts: List[Interval],
103	          iou_threshold: float = 0.5,
104	          match_result: Optional[MatchResult...

feat(eval): export tuned WASB rally segments for eyeball verification

Turns a cached WASB trajectory + a tuned threshold config into the actual rally
[start,end] segments that config produces, so the user can answer "are the
rallies actually better?" three ways: scrub the video to each segment, diff
tuned-vs-golden quantitatively, or cut clips.

- calibrate_wasb.make_predict_fn   -> trajectory -> segments for a config
- eval.metrics.match_intervals/score -> tuned-vs-golden IoU comparison
- tools.rally_slicer (--slice)       -> optional clip cutting; the exported CSV is
  written in the golden/slicer schema so it feeds straight back in

Outputs: a segments CSV (rally_number/start_time/end_time + mm:ss for scrubbing),
optionally a baseline-config CSV, and a golden-centric comparison CSV (per golden
rally: matched/MISS + IoU + boundary errors, plus extra/false-positive segments).
Default config is the tuned (0.05, 1.0, 1.0) from the first calibration; override
on the CLI. 5 new unit tests (no GPU/WSL); suite 187 green. On the real Sample
Long Video this reproduces the calibration exactly (F1 0.746, recall 0.880).

EOF

gh pr create --title "feat(eval): export tuned WASB rally segments for eyeball verification" --body "$(cat <<'EOF'
## Why
After calibration picked the tuned threshold config, we need to actually *see* whether the tuned rallies are better — not just trust the F1. This turns a cached WASB trajectory into the concrete rally `[start,end]` segments that config produces.

## What
`backend/eval/export_wasb_segments.py` — three ways to verify:
1. **Scrub** — a segments CSV with `rally_number,start_time,end_time` + `mm:ss` columns to jump to in the player.
2. **Diff** — a golden-centric comparison CSV: per golden rally → matched / MISS + IoU + boundary errors, plus extra (false-positive) segments.
3. **Cut** — `--slice --video <mp4>` writes one padded clip per tuned rally.

Reuses existing pieces rather than adding logic:
- `calibrate_wasb.make_predict_fn` — trajectory → segments for a config
- `eval.metrics.match_intervals/score` — tuned-vs-golden IoU comparison
- `tools.rally_slicer` — clip cutting; the exported CSV is written in the **golden/slicer schema**, so it feeds straight back in (covered by a round-trip test)

Default config is the tuned `(0.05, 1.0, 1.0)` from the first calibration; override via `--velocity-thresh/--merge-gap/--min-duration`.

## Validation
- 5 new unit tests (`tests/test_export_wasb_segments.py`, no GPU/WSL): mm:ss formatting, time-ordered numbering, **exported-CSV → rally_slicer round-trip**, match/miss/false-positive classification, end-to-end on a synthetic trajectory. Full suite **187 green**.
- Run on the **real** Sample Long Video trajectory + golden labels reproduces the calibration exactly: **34 tuned segments**, vs 25 golden → TP 22 / FN 3 / FP 12, **precision 0.647 · recall 0.880 · F1 0.746 · meanIoU 0.753**, mean boundary error start 0.64s / end 0.91s.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)

### 178. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.949Z)

All three tasks are done. Here's the wrap-up.

## ✅ What landed this session

**(4) Repo cleanup**
- Collector was stranded on the merged `feat/validate-delivery` branch → synced to `master`, merged branch deleted. Both repos genuinely clean now.
- **PR #11 closed (branch kept).** Mid-task I caught that my "orphaned, superseded by separate repo" framing was *wrong* — there is no separate khelacharya GitHub repo; that folder is a second clone of this same repo, and PR #11 was the deliberate way shot-intelligence work is kept off `master`. I surfaced that, you chose "close, keep branch," and I fixed the stale memory that misled me.

**(2) WASB-pass checkpointing → [PR #25](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/25) (merged, now on master)**
- The reboot-loses-everything bug is fixed. Detector outputs cache per-window to `<out>_wasbcache/det_raw.jsonl` (fsync'd every 1000 windows) + atomic manifest; resume skips ahead to the first un-cached window; the stateful tracker is decoupled and re-run over the full ordered cache. A lost trajectory CSV now recovers in seconds.
- WSL-validated: tracker-only re-run **byte-identical**; incremental resume matches the full run except 3/300 rows by ≤6×10⁻⁵ px (inherent cuDNN noise, not a bug). 11 new tests.

**(1) Export tuned segments → [PR #26](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/26) (open)**
- New `export_wasb_segments.py` reuses the windowing, metrics, and rally slicer. Ran on the **real** trajectory + golden labels. 5 new tests; suite 187 green.

## 👀 What to eyeball (the actual deliverable)

Artifacts in `output/` (gitignored): `wasb_tuned_segments.csv`, `_baseline.csv`, and the per-rally `wasb_tuned_segments_vs_golden.csv`.

**Tuned vs 25 golden rallies:** 22 matched, 3 "missed", 12 "extra" → precision 0.647 · recall 0.880 · **F1 0.746** (exactly reproduces the calibration). Honest read: recall is strong and boundaries are mostly <1s, but precision shows the tuned config **over-segments**. Several "errors" are artifacts, not hallucinations:

- **Fragmentation:** golden rally 3 (`0:44.5–0:51.1`) got split into two pieces (`44.8–46.9` + `48.4–50.8`), so it counts as 1 miss **+ 2 false positives from one real rally**. That's a `merge_gap` symptom.
- **Boundary too long:** golden 8 (`1:58.1`, a 1.5s rally) detected as `118.1–123.0` → 1 miss + 1 FP.
- **Short rally dropped:** golden 7 (`1:35.6–1:38.0`, 2.4s).
- **Boundary overruns to spot-check:** rally 1 starts 6.2s late, rally 21 ends 4.8s long, rally 23 ends 3.0s long.
- **~9 genuine extras to watch:** `0:03.4`, `0:36.9`, `0:53.9`, `1:00.5`, `1:19.5`, `1:47.2`, `2:24.5`, `3:08.2`, `5:39.1` — could be warm-up, between-point motion, or real unlabeled rallies.

If you want clips instead of scrubbing: `python -m backend.eval.export_wasb_segments --trajectory <traj> --video <mp4> --slice` cuts one padded clip per tuned rally.

## Open threads / your call
1. **Merge PR #26** when you're happy with it.
2. **Eyeball verdict** — after watching, the levers for better precision are: raise `merge_gap` (fixes the fragmentation), revisit `min_window_duration` (the dropped short rallies). The *real* precision verdict still needs a **2nd labelled video** (`leave_one_video_out`).

Memory/handoff updated throughout. Want me to take a pass at a `merge_gap` sweep to attack the fragmentation, or leave it until you've eyeballed?

22	def get_file_md5(filepath: str) -> str:
23	    total_size = os.path.getsize(filepath)
24	    hasher = hashlib.md5()
25	    processed = 0
26	    with open(filepath, "rb") as f:
27	        # Read in 1MB chunks for faster IO on huge files
28	        for chunk in iter(lambda: f.read(1024 * 1024), b""):
29	            hasher.update(chunk)
30	            processed += len(chunk)
31	            if processed % (1024 * 1024 * 50) == 0 or processed == total_size:
32	                print(f"\r[MD5] Processed {processed/(1024**3):.2f} GB / {total_size/(1024**3):.2f} GB ({(processed/total_size)*100:.1f}%)", end="", flush=True)
33	    print()
34	    return hasher.hexdigest()

### 217. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.951Z)

Read the real pipeline. One correction up front so we're aligned: I didn't check in any WASB weights this session — I checked in code (the resumable inference + the segment exporter). The actual weights are the pretrained wasb_badminton_best.pth.tar already sitting in WSL at ~/models/WASB-SBDT/pretrained_weights/. The demo will use those. Also worth knowing: the full wasb_hybrid segmenter's later phase hands each candidate rally to Gemini, which is the dead free-tier path we deliberately abandoned — so for this demo I'll run the shuttle-trajectory → windowing path directly (no AI, no stitcher), which is exactly the "segments as CSV" you're describing.

Here is the plan, plain text:

Inputs I need from you: the absolute path to the video file. That's it.

Step 0 — probe the video. I run ffprobe to read fps, frame width, duration, and frame count. These matter: the windowing normalizes shuttle velocity by frame width, and the segment times come from fps. I will NOT assume 60fps/1920 (those were the previous video's numbers). I'll report the probe back to you and a rough time estimate before the heavy step.

Step 1 — extract frames (fast path). I extract frames to JPGs in a temp folder using Windows ffmpeg (the proven fast route; the in-tool cv2 extractor is ~10x slower). Roughly 4GB and a few minutes for a ~6-minute clip; less for a shorter one. Deletable afterward.

Step 2 — WASB shuttle inference in WSL. I re-stage the current wasb_infer.py into the WSL WASB repo, activate the wasb conda env, and run inference over the frames with the pretrained badminton weights. Output is a trajectory CSV with columns Frame,Visibility,X,Y. This is the slow GPU step, but it now checkpoints — if anything interrupts it, re-running resumes instead of starting over. Expected throughput is about 27 windows/sec on this box, so I'll give you a concrete ETA after Step 0.

Step 3 — turn the trajectory into rally segments (no stitch, no AI). I run the exporter on the trajectory to produce the segment CSV: rally_number, start_time, end_time, duration, plus start/end as mm:ss so you can scrub directly. I'll generate two versions for comparison — one with the baseline thresholds (0.02 / 2.0 / 2.0) and one with the tuned thresholds (0.05 / 1.0 / 1.0) from the earlier calibration. Caveat I want to flag honestly: those tuned thresholds were fit on a different video (static-camera Sample Long Video), so on your new clip they're a starting point, not a guarantee — eyeballing both tells us whether they transfer.

Step 4 — hand off. I give you the CSV path(s) and a short summary (segment count, total seconds of play). No video gets stitched or written. You eyeball the source video against the timestamps and note observations.

What I will NOT do: no Gemini/AI handoff, no stitcher/compiler, no database writes, no committing the trajectory or frames (artifacts stay local/gitignored).

Two quick choices for you, or just say "go with defaults":
- Config: both baseline and tuned (my default), or only one.
- Frame coords: the CSV uses time only (seconds + mm:ss); say if you also want per-rally peak/mean shuttle velocity columns to help judge borderline segments.

Drop the video path and your go-ahead and I'll start with the probe.

### 280. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.955Z)

Fun one. Let me Fermi it honestly — the per-ramp numbers I can defend; the fleet-wide number depends on Anthropic's user counts, which I don't have, so I'll keep that part as a plug-in-your-own formula rather than pretend I know it.

The unit being saved: one cold ramp-up = the DFS/BFS I'd otherwise do (a flurry of Glob/Grep/Read) replaced by a single ~5k-token map read.

Per cold ramp-up:
- Avoided input tokens (file contents + tool results dragged into context): ~50k–120k, call it ~80k.
- Avoided output tokens (the multi-turn "what do I open next" reasoning + tool-call args): ~5k–15k, call it ~8k. This is the *expensive* slice — output tokens cost ~4–5× input on price and are far heavier in FLOPs (autoregressive, one forward pass per token vs. a single batched prefill for input).
- Minus the map read (~5k in). Net ≈ 75k input + 8k output saved, and ~20–40 fewer tool round-trips (~30–90s less wall-clock).

Fleet math (parametric — these inputs are illustrative, not Anthropic data):
saved ≈ S·U·R, where S ≈ 85k tokens/ramp, U = weekly-active codebase users, R ≈ 8 cold ramps/user/week.
- U = 100k → ~68B tokens/week (~3.5T/yr)
- U = 1M → ~680B/week (~35T/yr)
- U = 10M → ~6.8T/week (~350T/yr)

CPU/GPU: tokens are the proxy for inference FLOPs. The ~8k saved *output* tokens per ramp is where the GPU-seconds live; at the 1M-user line that's ~64B output tokens/week not generated — real datacenter-hours, and it's the compounding kind because every avoided tool turn also avoids re-prefilling the growing context.

Network: ~80k tokens ≈ ~300–500 KB of text per ramp that never crosses the tool↔model↔client hops, plus the round-trip/latency tax of 25-ish calls. At 1M users that's ~3–4 TB/week — modest next to the token bill, but the latency win is what a human actually feels.

The honest caveats, because the headline number flatters itself:
- Prompt caching already kills most *intra-session* re-reads; the real prize is *cross-session cold starts* and post-compaction re-exploration.
- The map isn't free: generating a good one costs ~150k–500k tokens (this very workflow is in that range), and it drifts, so subtract regeneration/incremental-update cost. Break-even is ~2–6 reads — trivial for a repo you "frequently open," which is exactly your framing.
- Not every session ramps cold; warm continuations get nothing.
- It partly exists already — CLAUDE.md, `/init`, repo memory all chase this. The novel bit here is a *dense, anchor-rich, machine-first* map plus a *deterministic regen pipeline*, so it stays true instead of rotting into stale prose.

So: per-ramp, a clean ~10× reduction in ramp cost (85k → ~5k tokens, 25 tool calls → 1). Fleet-wide, "tens of trillions of tokens a year" is a defensible order of magnitude at 1M+ heavy users — dominated economically by the small output-token slice, not the big input one. Both background jobs still cooking; I'll surface them as they land.

### 321. user (2026-07-02T06:11:32.958Z)

can you run the baseline, which is without these tuned weights? And tell the %age improvement? I have eyeballed, some are really good rallies detected, but some have the pattern which I am documenting, now we are overfiring because it is detecting the player sending the shuttle to another for service after losing on an unforced error or missing the shuttle, is detected as a rally start. We need to identify a way to say "When both players are in "start stance i.e. one serving and one side receiving for singles or doubles" only then start a rally" or else it is overdetecting them. I know we are relying on a "shuttle trajectory" model so when the player send the shuttle by throwing it or hitting it by raquet, it probably shows up as a trajectory and gets detected. We need also a state i.e. "if rally end is detected,  then again wait for the start stance rule" or something, probably needs some research now.

description: Run baseline + compare baseline vs tuned on adarsh trajectory
command: cd "C:/Users/avidu/Projects/badminton-highlight-indexer"
python - <<'PY'
from backend.eval.calibrate_wasb import make_predict_fn, BASELINE
from backend.eval.export_wasb_segments import TUNED, write_segments_csv
from backend.eval.metrics import match_intervals

traj = r"C:\Users\avidu\AppData\Local\Temp\adarsh_traj.csv"
pf = make_predict_fn(traj, fps=59.94005994, frame_width=1920)
b, t = pf(BASELINE), pf(TUNED)
write_segments_csv("output/adarsh_baseline_segments.csv", b)

tot = lambda s: sum(e - st for st, e in s)
durs = lambda s: sorted(round(e - st, 1) for st, e in s)
print(f"BASELINE {BASELINE}: {len(b)} segments, {tot(b):.1f}s play, mean {tot(b)/len(b):.1f}s")
print(f"TUNED    {TUNED}: {len(t)} segments, {tot(t):.1f}s play, mean {tot(t)/len(t):.1f}s")
print(f"delta: tuned has {len(t)-len(b):+d} segments ({100*(len(t)-len(b))/len(b):+.0f}%), "
      f"{tot(t)-tot(b):+.1f}s play ({100*(tot(t)-tot(b))/tot(b):+.0f}%)")

# tuned-vs-baseline structural diff (neither is GT): tuned-only = the extra fires tuned introduces
mr = match_intervals(t, b, 0.5)
tuned_only = [t[i] for i in mr.unmatched_pred_indices]
base_only = [b[i] for i in mr.unmatched_gt_indices]
print(f"\noverlap (IoU>=0.5): {len(mr.matches)} shared")
print(f"TUNED-ONLY (extra vs baseline) = {len(tuned_only)} segs; durations: {durs(tuned_only)}")
print(f"BASELINE-ONLY (tuned merged/dropped) = {len(base_only)} segs; durations: {durs(base_only)}")
# how many tuned segments are <=2s (the short single-toss pattern the user flagged)
short = [s for s in t if s[1]-s[0] <= 2.0]
print(f"\nTUNED short (<=2.0s, the suspected service-feed false fires): {len(short)} of {len(t)}")
PY

### 325. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.958Z)

Baseline's in, and the comparison is genuinely illuminating — it confirms your eyeball diagnosis hard.

**Baseline (0.02/2.0/2.0) vs Tuned (0.05/1.0/1.0) on Adarsh — `output/adarsh_baseline_segments.csv`:**

| | segments | play time | mean len |
|---|---|---|---|
| Baseline | 45 | 303.8 s | 6.8 s |
| Tuned | 65 | 251.4 s | 3.9 s |

Tuned is **+20 segments (+44%)** but **−52 s play (−17%)**, and the structural diff shows *why* that's bad, not good:
- **30 tuned-only segments**, durations almost all **1.0–2.0 s** (19 of 65 are ≤2 s) — these are your between-point service-feed false fires.
- **10 baseline-only segments** that tuned *lost* — and they're the **long rallies** (17.7 s, 10.9 s, 9.6 s, 9.2 s, 8.9 s, 7.6 s). Tuned's `merge_gap=1.0` **fragments real long rallies** so they no longer survive as single segments.

So on the honest read: **there's no "% improvement" here — if anything tuned regresses on this video.** No golden labels means I can't put a precision/F1 number on it, but the pattern is clear: the tuned thresholds were *overfit to the Sample Long Video* and don't transfer (this is exactly the `leave_one_video_out` caution made concrete). For Adarsh, **baseline is the better operating point** — fewer false fires, long rallies kept whole.

---

**The over-firing problem — your diagnosis is right, and there's a clean way to attack it.**

The data backs you: every false fire is a *short single trajectory* (the loser flicking/tossing the shuttle back for service registers as a 1–2 s "rally"). A real rally has the shuttle crossing the net **multiple times**; a service feed crosses **once**. That gives a spectrum of fixes, cheap → robust, all grounded in what we already have:

- **(A) Shuttle-only rally signature — no new model, highest ROI.** Gate each candidate on **net/midline crossings ≥ K** (≈ shot count) or trajectory direction-reversals, not just duration. A single toss = 1 crossing → rejected; a real exchange = ≥2–3 → kept. Implementable purely on the WASB trajectory we already produce. This alone should kill most of the 30 false fires.

- **(B) Your start-stance state machine — uses the person detector we already have.** WASB is shuttle-only, but the repo already ships a person detector (`yolo_hybrid`: torchvision Faster R-CNN + ByteTrack). At a legal serve both players are **stationary and diagonally positioned** in the service zones; between points they're **moving** (picking up the shuttle, repositioning). So: a state machine `IDLE → (await start-stance: players settled + diagonal) → ARMED → (shuttle trajectory) → RALLY → (rally end) → IDLE`. A trajectory that fires while *not ARMED* is rejected. Efficient version: **WASB proposes candidates cheaply; a stance-gate runs the person detector only in the ~2 s around each candidate start** to accept/reject — no full-video person pass needed (that path was ~50 min/video, which is why it was abandoned for proposal but is fine as a *verifier*).

- **(C) Pose-based serve detection — richest, new model + licensing review.** Detect the actual serve posture. Highest fidelity, most cost; probably overkill unless A+B fall short.

My recommendation: **A now** (fast, kills the documented pattern, shuttle-only), with **B as the robust target**. C only if needed. This needs a bit of research to do B right (how the badminton-CV literature — ShuttleSet, TrackNet-adjacent work — actually detects serves/rally boundaries), which is what you're sensing.

Let me capture this finding and then let you steer the next step.

description: Measure crossing gate on Adarsh baseline+tuned
command: cd "C:/Users/avidu/Projects/badminton-highlight-indexer"
python - <<'PY'
from backend.detectors.tracknet_runner import TrackNetRunner
from backend.detectors import rally_gate as rg
from backend.eval.calibrate_wasb import make_predict_fn, BASELINE
from backend.eval.export_wasb_segments import TUNED, write_segments_csv

traj = r"C:\Users\avidu\AppData\Local\Temp\adarsh_traj.csv"
fps = 59.94005994
pts = TrackNetRunner.parse_trajectory_csv(traj)
axis, net, span = rg.estimate_play_axis(pts)
print(f"play axis={axis} net={net:.0f} span={span:.0f} (deadband={0.06*span:.0f}px)")

pf = make_predict_fn(traj, fps=fps, frame_width=1920)
for name, cfg in [("BASELINE", BASELINE), ("TUNED", TUNED)]:
    wins = pf(cfg)
    g = rg.gate_windows(pts, wins, fps, min_crossings=2)
    cc = sorted(x.crossings for x in g)
    print(f"\n{name} {cfg}: {len(wins)} windows | crossing counts: {cc}")
    for K in (1, 2, 3):
        kept = [x for x in g if x.crossings >= K]
        killed_short = sum(1 for x in g if x.crossings < K and (x.end - x.start) <= 2.0)
        killed_long = sum(1 for x in g if x.crossings < K and (x.end - x.start) > 4.0)
        play = sum(x.end - x.start for x in kept)
        print(f"  K>={K}: keep {len(kept)}/{len(wins)} ({play:.0f}s) | kills {killed_short} short(<=2s) + {killed_long} long(>4s)")

# write the recommended: BASELINE + gate(K=2)
base = pf(BASELINE)
kept = rg.apply_gate(pts, base, fps, min_crossings=2)
write_segments_csv("output/adarsh_baseline_gated.csv", kept)
print(f"\nwrote output/adarsh_baseline_gated.csv : BASELINE+gate(K=2) = {len(kept)} segments")
PY
[/external_agent_tool_call]

feat(detectors): rally-start gate — suppress between-point false fires (gate A)

WASB (shuttle-only) over-detects: a player flicking/tossing the shuttle back to the
opponent for service after losing a point is a single trajectory that looks like a
rally. The discriminator is structural, not duration-based: a real rally has the
shuttle crossing the net MANY times (each shot lands on the other side); a single
service feed crosses ~once.

backend/detectors/rally_gate.py (pure, camera-agnostic, NO new model): estimate the
play axis (image axis with the larger shuttle spread) and the net (median shuttle
coord on that axis), then count sign changes of (coord - net) across the visible
points in a window, with a deadband to ignore net-line jitter. Gate windows on
crossings >= K. Wired into export_wasb_segments via --min-crossings (0=off).

Measured on the Adarsh demo trajectory (side camera, auto-detected): real rallies
score 5-13 crossings, junk scores 0-1. At K>=2 the gate kills 18 of ~19 short
(<=2s) tuned false fires while keeping every long rally; on baseline it trims 7
low-crossing windows (start-of-clip junk, one-sided non-rallies). This is gate A in
the over-fire fix spectrum; B (player-stance state machine using the existing person
detector as a verifier) is the robust follow-up.

6 new tests (rally_gate + export integration); suite 193 green.

## Why
WASB is shuttle-only, so the loser flicking/tossing the shuttle back for service registers as a 1–2 s "rally" (the over-firing pattern observed while eyeballing the Adarsh demo). Threshold tuning can't fix this cleanly — it's structural.

## What
`backend/detectors/rally_gate.py` — a pure, **camera-agnostic, no-new-model** gate. Real rallies cross the net many times (each shot lands on the other side); a single service feed crosses ~once. It estimates the play axis (image axis with larger shuttle spread → handles baseline *and* side cameras) and the net (median shuttle coord), then counts sign changes of `(coord − net)` across visible points in a window, with a deadband for net-line jitter. Keep windows with `crossings ≥ K`.

Wired into `export_wasb_segments.py` via `--min-crossings` (0 = off, **2 recommended**).

## Measured on the Adarsh demo (side camera, auto-detected)
- Real rallies score **5–13 crossings**; junk scores **0–1**.
- `K≥2` on **tuned** kills **18 of ~19** short (≤2 s) false fires, keeps every long rally.
- `K≥2` on **baseline** trims 7 low-crossing windows (start-of-clip junk, 0-crossing one-sided non-rallies) → 45 → 38 segments.

## Scope
Gate **A** in the fix spectrum. Gate **B** (player-stance state machine — `IDLE → await-stance → ARMED → RALLY → end`, reusing the existing `yolo_hybrid` person detector as a verifier around candidate starts) is the robust follow-up and needs a research pass. Currently exposed only via the export tool; folding into `trajectory_to_action_windows` for the live segmenter is a later step.

6 new tests; suite **193 green**.

### 375. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.961Z)

**Gate A is shipped** — [PR #28](https://github.com/avidullu/badminton-highlight-indexer/pull/28), `output/adarsh_baseline_gated.csv` is your "stronger baseline now" (45→38, junk removed, all long rallies kept). One window to eyeball: 4:39.5–4:44.7. master clean, suite 193 green.

---

**Pondering the paid-Gemini-as-fallback idea — it's a good instinct, and the architecture is already shaped for it.**

The key thing you may not realize: **the hybrid pipeline already does exactly the cost-bounded thing you're reaching for.** `wasb_hybrid` Phase 3 doesn't send the whole video to Gemini — it sends *only the padded candidate clips* (one short clip per WASB candidate window). So the Gemini bill scales with **#candidates × clip-length, not video duration.** It "died" earlier purely on free-tier quota (20 req/day), not on architecture. A paid key revives it as-is — you don't even need to pre-slice the whole video to <1 GB; the candidate clips are 2–15 s each, far under any upload limit. (The <1 GB instinct still matters if you ever hand Gemini a *whole* match.)

So the cost-optimal stack writes itself, and gate A makes it cheaper:

> **WASB proposes** (cheap, recall-first) → **gate A** (free, removes the obvious junk → *fewer Gemini calls*) → **Gemini verifies the survivors** (paid, precision + tightens boundaries + can reject a between-point feed because it actually *sees* it's not a rally).

Gate A and Gemini aren't competing — A shrinks the Gemini bill. On Adarsh that's ~38 short clips to verify instead of 65+, i.e., a modest per-video cost (bounded by clip count, not the 11-minute runtime). "Calculated bit-expensive," exactly your framing.

Two strategic angles worth separating:

1. **Stronger baseline *now* (before next week's human labels):** point paid Gemini at your *owned* test videos (Adarsh et al.) to generate a **silver ground-truth** cheaply. That gives you something the threshold-tuning is currently flying blind without — an actual precision/recall number on this video — so we can *measure* gate A and the thresholds instead of eyeballing. Caveat from your own oracle rule ([[golden-set-vendoring]]): an auto-labeler must be a *different lineage* than production — Gemini vs WASB qualifies, so it's a legitimate independent oracle, but treat its labels as **silver, not gold**. Next week's human annotations stay authoritative; Gemini is the bridge until then.

2. **Product fallback tier (real users):** here's the one tension to hold — sending user video to Gemini **breaks the privacy lever** you'd banked on ([[monetization-plan]]: "vendors/AI never see user uploads"). So the clean design is **default = CV-only (WASB + gate A): private, fast, cheap; premium/opt-in = + Gemini verification: higher quality, costs money, leaves your infra.** That keeps the privacy promise as the default and makes AI a *consented* quality upgrade — which is also a natural pricing boundary.

**Concrete next step I can take** (needs a paid `GEMINI_API_KEY` in env from you): wire and run the **gate-A → Gemini-verify** path on the Adarsh candidates. You'd get (a) a sharper Adarsh segment set immediately, and (b) a silver-GT to actually quantify gate A's precision/recall before the human labels land. Say the word and set the key, and I'll build it. Otherwise I'll hold Gemini for the tier design and we let gate A + next week's golden videos drive the numbers.

Open PRs to merge when you're ready: **#27** (code map), **#28** (gate A).

41	        except Exception as e:
42	            logger.error(f"[{log_prefix}] Upload failed: {e}")
43	            return [], metrics

50	                time.sleep(10)

1	import subprocess
2	import os
3	import shutil
4	import logging
5	
6	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
7	
8	def get_video_duration(video_path: str) -> float:
9	    """Returns the exact duration of a video file in seconds using ffprobe.
10	    Returns 0.0 on failure."""
11	    try:
12	        duration_output = subprocess.check_output([
13	            "ffprobe", "-v", "error", "-show_entries", "format=duration",
14	            "-of", "default=noprint_wrappers=1:nokey=1", video_path
15	        ]).decode().strip()
16	        return float(duration_output)
17	    except Exception as e:
18	        logger.error(f"Could not detect video duration for {video_path}: {e}")
19	        return 0.0
20	
21	def extract_video_chunk(video_path: str, start_time: float, duration: float,
22	                        output_path: str, reencode: bool = False) -> bool:
23	    """Extracts a chunk from a video file.
24	
25	    Args:
26	        reencode: If True, re-encodes with libx264 (slower but frame-accurate).
27	                  If False, uses stream-copy (fast but cuts at nearest keyframe).
28	                  Use True for short clips where keyframe drift matters.
29	    """
30	    cmd = [
31	        "ffmpeg", "-y",
32	        "-ss", str(start_time),
33	        "-t", str(duration),
34	        "-i", video_path,
35	    ]
36	    if reencode:
37	        cmd += ["-c:v", "libx264", "-preset", "ultrafast", "-crf", "23", "-c:a", "aac"]
38	    else:
39	        cmd += ["-c", "copy"]
40	    cmd.append(output_path)
41	    try:
42	        subprocess.run(cmd, check=True, stdout=subprocess.DEVNULL, stderr=subprocess.DEVNULL)
43	        return True
44	    except Exception as e:
45	        logger.error(f"Failed to extract chunk {start_time}s - {start_time+duration}s: {e}")
46	        return False
47

### 409. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.964Z)

Smoke test caught two real things before I spent the full run — exactly why I ran it:

1. **A transient upload failure** (`WinError 10053`) killed cand 0 — the provider has no upload retry.
2. **More important: Gemini fragments a long rally in a short clip.** Cand 3 (a real 10.2 s rally) came back as two ~1.5 s pieces, which `min_dur` then *dropped entirely* — i.e., the naive per-candidate approach can lose good long rallies (the ones WASB nails).

Both are fixable cheaply. Fix (1): add upload retry to the existing provider (a real robustness win, not a workaround). Fix (2): since each WASB candidate ≈ one rally, take the **envelope** (min-start→max-end) of Gemini's segments per candidate instead of treating fragments as separate rallies. Let me apply both.

### 520. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.970Z)

Love it — and let me be straight about where the precision lever actually is: Claude subagents can't watch the video better than Gemini, so spawning them to *judge* rallies adds nothing. The real precision gains come from **how I orchestrate Gemini**, and there are two concrete levers I haven't pulled yet:

1. **Full-context pass** — the candidate-refine is capped by WASB's recall (TestLargeVideo only proposed 5 windows; rally 1 was a loose 17 s window Gemini had to trim to 2.7 s). Sending Gemini the *whole* clip (downscaled, under your 500 MB rule) lets it find rallies WASB never proposed and set boundaries with full context.
2. **Ensemble voting** — judge each rally with N independent Gemini calls, keep the majority and use median boundaries → rejects borderline false positives.

Let me build the full-context pass first (cheapest, biggest likely gain), run it on TestLargeVideo, and put it side-by-side with the candidate-refine so you can eyeball which is better.

### 539. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.971Z)

This is the precision win, and it exposes a real flaw in the candidate-refine. Side-by-side for TestLargeVideo:

| Candidate-refine (5) | Full-context (7) |
|---|---|
| 1: 0:08.8–0:11.5 | 1: 0:08.5–0:11.5 ✓ |
| **2: 0:24.4–0:46.6 (one 22 s blob)** | **2: 0:23.5–0:26.5** |
| | **3: 0:31.5–0:33.5** |
| | **4: 0:39.0–0:42.5** |
| 3: 0:49.2–0:54.7 | 5: 0:49.0–0:54.5 ✓ |
| 4: 1:13.9–1:26.9 | 6: 1:13.5–1:27.0 ✓ |
| 5: 1:35.1–1:40.7 | 7: 1:35.5–1:39.5 ✓ |

**The decisive difference is the 0:23–0:43 stretch.** Candidate-refine reported it as **one 22 s rally**; full-context says it's actually **three separate rallies** (0:23.5–0:26.5, 0:31.5–0:33.5, 0:39.0–0:42.5) with dead time between them.

Root cause: WASB's `merge_gap=2.0` bridged the short between-point gaps into one 24 s candidate, and my **envelope** logic then collapsed it into a single 22 s "rally" — sweeping ~13 s of dead time into a rally. That's exactly a precision loss. **Full-context Gemini, seeing the whole clip, correctly splits it.** Everything else the two agree on; full-context is a strict improvement (same rallies, tighter, plus the correct split).

**Here's where I need your eyes** (the one check that settles it): scrub **0:23 → 0:43**. Is that **one continuous rally** (refine right) or **three rallies with breaks** (full-context right)? I'm confident it's three — if you confirm, full-context becomes the precision-improved default and I'll drop the over-merging envelope.

Let me record the finding while you look.

description: Commit + push Gemini stack
command: cd "C:/Users/avidu/Projects/badminton-highlight-indexer"
git checkout -b feat/gemini-refine 2>&1 | tail -1
git add backend/eval/gemini_refine.py tests/test_gemini_refine.py backend/providers/gemini.py tests/test_provider_gemini.py backend/utils/video.py
git commit -q -F - <<'EOF'
feat(eval): Gemini precision pass for WASB rallies (refine + full-context)

Use paid Gemini to raise rally-detection precision on top of the WASB shuttle
substrate. Two modes in backend/eval/gemini_refine.py (reuses GeminiProvider +
badminton prompt + extract_video_chunk + make_predict_fn + write_segments_csv —
no reinvented Gemini/prompt/windowing logic):

- refine (WASB candidates -> Gemini): cut each WASB candidate to a short padded,
  downscaled clip; Gemini returns the true rally inside or rejects it. Cheap
  (seconds-long clips). Recall bounded by WASB's proposals.
- full-context (--full-video): hand Gemini the whole clip (chunked <=120s,
  downscaled), so it finds every rally itself — not bounded by WASB recall.

On the 5.3K TestLargeVideo, full-context beat refine decisively and was
user-verified: refine collapsed a 0:23-0:43 stretch into one 22s "rally" (the old
envelope-per-candidate over-merged three real rallies WASB had bundled into one
candidate); full-context correctly split them into 24-26 / 31-34 / 39-42. Fixed:
dropped the envelope; tiny shuttle-invisibility splits are now healed by a
gap-tolerant merge (<=1s) while genuine between-rally gaps stay separate.

Provider hardening (backend/providers/gemini.py): retry transient upload + 503
generate failures (3x backoff); refuse uploads over MAX_UPLOAD_MB=500 before
sending (2GB API limit, with margin). extract_video_chunk gains scale_width to
downscale uploads (Gemini analyses at low res anyway -> small/fast/under cap, no
analysis loss) — essential for 4K/5K sources.

Model note: gemini-2.5-flash 503s under load, gemini-2.0-flash is 404 (deprecated);
default is gemini-flash-latest (current stable flash, validated clean). Each worker
uses its own client (genai client isn't thread-safe). 7 new tests; suite 192 green.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 4.8 <noreply@anthropic.com>
EOF
git push -u origin feat/gemini-refine 2>&1 | tail -3
[/external_agent_tool_call]

## Why
WASB (shuttle-only) over-fires (between-point service feeds) and its `merge_gap` bundles multiple rallies into one candidate. Use paid Gemini to raise **precision** on top of the WASB substrate.

## What — `backend/eval/gemini_refine.py` (reuses existing wheels: GeminiProvider, badminton prompt, `extract_video_chunk`, `make_predict_fn`, `write_segments_csv`)
- **refine** (WASB candidates → Gemini): short padded, downscaled clip per candidate → Gemini returns the true rally or rejects it. Cheap; recall bounded by WASB.

## Validated (user-confirmed) on 5.3K TestLargeVideo
Full-context **beat** refine and matched the human eyeball: refine collapsed 0:23–0:43 into one 22 s "rally"; full-context correctly split it into **24–26 / 31–34 / 39–42**. Root cause was the old **envelope-per-candidate** over-merging three real rallies WASB bundled into one candidate. **Fix:** dropped the envelope; tiny shuttle-invisibility splits are healed by a **gap-tolerant merge (≤1 s)** while genuine between-rally gaps stay separate.

## Provider hardening (`backend/providers/gemini.py`)
- Retry transient **upload** + **503** generate failures (3× backoff).
- Refuse uploads over **`MAX_UPLOAD_MB=500`** before sending (2 GB API limit, with margin).
- `extract_video_chunk` gains **`scale_width`** to downscale uploads (Gemini analyses at low res anyway → small/fast/under-cap, no analysis loss) — essential for 4K/5K sources.

## Model note
`gemini-2.5-flash` 503s under load; `gemini-2.0-flash` is 404 (deprecated) — default is **`gemini-flash-latest`** (current stable flash, validated clean). Each worker gets its own client (genai client isn't thread-safe).

### 574. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.973Z)

Yes — that makes complete sense, and it's the right framing. Let me say it back to be sure we're aligned:

**Gemini is the offline *teacher*; the *local-only* WASB + heuristics path is the *student* that ships.** I (the orchestrator) use expensive cloud Gemini *once, offline* to produce silver-ground-truth rally labels, then **calibrate the local-only knobs** (velocity / merge-gap / min-duration **+ gate-A net-crossings**) to match Gemini as closely as possible — measured honestly across videos. Production then runs **100% local** (no Gemini, no upload, privacy lever intact, ~zero marginal cost); Gemini's quality is "distilled" into the thresholds. That's exactly the privacy/cost design you've been pointing at.

And the timing is perfect: with TestLargeVideo + Adarsh both getting Gemini labels, I finally have **2 videos** → I can do honest **leave-one-video-out** (tune on one, test on the other) instead of single-video overfitting.

Let me check the Adarsh teacher-label run and then build the local-vs-Gemini calibrator.

1	"""Quantify rally-segmentation improvement — and guard against overfitting.
2	
3	This is the measurement layer for per-video / per-corpus calibration. It answers
4	two questions honestly:
5	
6	1. **"Did precision improve, by how much?"** — `improvement_report` scores a
7	   baseline config vs a tuned config on the same ground truth and reports the
8	   before/after metrics and the % delta.
9	
10	2. **"Is that gain real or overfit?"** — calibration must be measured on data the
11	   tuning never saw, otherwise you're just memorising. Two protocols:
12	   - `cross_validate` — k-fold over one video's rallies: pick the config on the
13	     train folds, score on the held-out fold. The **generalization_gap**
14	     (train − test) is the overfit alarm.
15	   - `leave_one_video_out` — the protocol that matters as more videos arrive:
16	     pick the config on all *other* videos, score on the held-out video. Reports
17	     per-video held-out scores + mean±std. This is the number to trust.
18	
19	Everything is segmenter-agnostic: callers pass a `predict_fn(config) ->
20	List[Interval]` (e.g. WASB trajectory → windows at those thresholds), so the same
21	harness works for WASB, yolo_hybrid, or anything else. Pure + unit-tested; no I/O.
22	"""
23	from __future__ import annotations
24	
25	import statistics as _st
26	from typing import Callable, Dict, List, Sequence, Tuple, Any
27	
28	from backend.eval.metrics import score, Score, Interval
29	
30	Config = Any                              # opaque to this module (e.g. a thresholds dict)
31	PredictFn = Callable[[Config], List[Interval]]
32	
33	
34	def pct_improvement(before: float, after: float) -> float:
35	    """Percentage change from `before` to `after` (e.g. 0.6 -> 0.75 == +25.0)."""
36	    if before <= 0:
37	        return float("inf") if after > 0 else 0.0
38	    return (after - before) / before * 100.0
39	
40	
41	def evaluate(preds: List[Interval], gts: List[Interval], iou_threshold: float = 0.5) -> Score:
42	    """Precision / recall / F1 / mean-IoU of predicted vs ground-truth intervals."""
43	    return score(preds, gts, iou_threshold)
44	
45	
46	def kfold_indices(n: int, k: int) -> List[Tuple[List[int], List[int]]]:
47	    """Deterministic k-fold split of indices 0..n-1 into (train_idx, test_idx)."""
48	    if n <= 0:
49	        return []
50	    k = max(2, min(k, n))                 # need >=2 folds; cap at n
51	    folds = [list(range(i, n, k)) for i in range(k)]   # round-robin → balanced, deterministic
52	    splits = []
53	    for f in range(k):
54	        test = folds[f]
55	        train = [i for i in range(n) if i not in set(test)]
56	        if test and train:
57	            splits.append((train, test))
58	    return splits
59	
60	
61	def select_best(predict_fn: PredictFn, configs: Sequence[Config], gts: List[Interval],
62	                metric: str = "f1", iou_threshold: float = 0.5) -> Tuple[Config, float]:
63	    """Pick the config whose predictions maximise `metric` against `gts`."""
64	    best_cfg, best_val = None, -1.0
65	    for cfg in configs:
66	        val = getattr(score(predict_fn(cfg), gts, iou_threshold), metric)
67	        if val > best_val:
68	            best_cfg, best_val = cfg, val
69	    return best_cfg, best_val
70	
71	
72	def cross_validate(predict_fn: PredictFn, configs: Sequence[Config], gts: List[Interval],
73	                   k: int = 5, metric: str = "recall", iou_threshold: float = 0.5) -> Dict[str, Any]:
74	    """k-fold threshold calibration on ONE video's rallies (within-video overfit guard).
75	
76	    Per fold: choose the best config on the train rallies, score it on the held-out
77	    rallies. A large `generalization_gap` (train >> test) means the thresholds are
78	    overfitting that video's quirks.
79	
80	    Default metric is **recall** on purpose: predictions are global (whole-video
81	    windows), so scoring them against a *fold subset* of GT would unfairly count the
82	    other rallies' windows as false positives — pre...

### 606. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.975Z)

This is the honest, important result — and it changes the recommendation. Two runs, F1-optimized and precision-optimized:

**The local pipeline, measured against Gemini for the first time (held-out / leave-one-video-out — the trustworthy number):**

| | baseline (untuned) | calibrated | overfit gap |
|---|---|---|---|
| **F1-optimized** | 0.438 | **0.483** ±0.17 | +0.035 ✅ small |
| **Precision-optimized** | 0.444 | **0.320** ↓ ±0.10 | +0.245 ⚠️ huge |

Read carefully, because it's not the rosy answer:

- **F1-tuning gives a small, honest lift** (0.44 → 0.48, low overfit gap). Real but modest.
- **Precision-tuning *overfits badly*** — held-out precision actually **drops** (0.44 → 0.32) with a +0.245 gap. Chasing precision on the threshold grid memorizes each video's quirks and **doesn't transfer**. The two videos even want opposite configs (Adarsh likes aggressive gating `min_crossings=3`; TestLargeVideo likes none).
- **Why:** only **2 videos, and very dissimilar** (5.3K phone clip vs the Adarsh setup), so leave-one-video-out is noisy and transfer is weak. TestLargeVideo is also **recall-capped** — WASB proposed ~5 windows but Gemini found 7, so the local pipeline can't exceed ~5/7 there no matter the thresholds.

**Honest conclusion:** the local-only pipeline agrees with Gemini at only ~0.44 precision/F1, and **threshold-tuning alone hits a ceiling fast** (and overfits for precision on so few videos). The real levers for local precision are:
1. **More teacher-labeled videos** (next week) → stable LOVO, configs that actually transfer.
2. **A learned distilled model** — train the existing `classifier.py` on Gemini labels using candidate features (peak/mean velocity, active frames, **net-crossings**) instead of 4 hand thresholds. This can capture patterns thresholds can't, and is the proper "distill Gemini into local."
3. **Better WASB recall** (raise the candidate ceiling).

The calibration *framework* is the valuable deliverable here (it's the teacher-student measurement harness, and it'll only get better as videos arrive). Let me record this and commit it, then I'd recommend the **learned-distillation** path over more threshold-tuning.

[external_agent_tool_result]
1	"""Build a training set for the offline, API-free learned rally segmenter.
2	
3	Phase 4 of the collector->indexer bridge (see docs/ROADMAP.md, ADR-004). The idea:
4	a detector (YOLO player-motion today, optionally WASB shuttle-motion later)
5	produces candidate rally windows with features; the collector's ground-truth
6	rally intervals tell us which windows are real. A lightweight classifier learns
7	that mapping, replacing the paid Gemini refinement with a local model.
8	
9	This module is the *data layer* — pure and unit-tested:
10	
11	  - `label_candidates`  — generic: label windows positive/negative by whether they
12	    match a ground-truth rally (same IoU matching the evaluator uses, so training
13	    labels are consistent with how we score).
14	  - `extract_yolo_features` — YOLO-specific feature vector from a candidate row's
15	    persisted `stats`. Pluggable: a WASB substrate supplies its own feature_fn.
16	  - `build_training_set` — glue: rows + GT -> (X, y, intervals).
17	
18	No model training or I/O here; that lives in the trainer/segmenter once the
19	substrate is chosen from the Phase 3 candidate-recall result.
20	"""
21	
22	from typing import Callable, Dict, List, Tuple
23	
24	from backend.eval.metrics import Interval, match_intervals
25	
26	# YOLO Stage-1 persists these per candidate window in `candidate_segments.stats`
27	# (see HybridYoloSegmenter). Duration is derived from the window bounds.
28	YOLO_FEATURE_NAMES = [
29	    "duration", "peak_velocity", "mean_velocity", "active_frame_count", "track_id_count",
30	]
31	
32	
33	def extract_yolo_features(row: Dict) -> List[float]:
34	    """Feature vector for one YOLO candidate row (dict from query_candidate_segments).
35	
36	    `stats` is already JSON-deserialized by the DB layer. Missing fields default
37	    to 0.0 so a sparse/old row still yields a well-formed vector.
38	    """
39	    stats = row.get("stats") or {}
40	    duration = float(row["end_time"]) - float(row["start_time"])
41	    return [
42	        duration,
43	        float(stats.get("peak_velocity", 0.0)),
44	        float(stats.get("mean_velocity", 0.0)),
45	        float(stats.get("active_frame_count", 0)),
46	        float(stats.get("track_id_count", 0)),
47	    ]
48	
49	
50	def label_candidates(candidate_intervals: List[Interval], gt_intervals: List[Interval],
51	                     iou_threshold: float = 0.5) -> List[int]:
52	    """Label each candidate window 1 (real rally) or 0 (spurious).
53	
54	    A window is positive iff it is matched one-to-one to a ground-truth rally at
55	    `iou_threshold` — the *same* matching the evaluator uses for true positives,
56	    so the classifier is trained on exactly what we score. Substrate-agnostic:
57	    works for YOLO or WASB-derived windows alike.
58	    """
59	    mr = match_intervals(candidate_intervals, gt_intervals, iou_threshold)
60	    positive = {m.pred_index for m in mr.matches}
61	    return [1 if i in positive else 0 for i in range(len(candidate_intervals))]
62	
63	
64	def build_training_set(
65	    candidate_rows: List[Dict],
66	    gt_intervals: List[Interval],
67	    iou_threshold: float = 0.5,
68	    feature_fn: Callable[[Dict], List[float]] = extract_yolo_features,
69	) -> Tuple[List[List[float]], List[int], List[Interval]]:
70	    """Turn one video's candidate rows + ground truth into (X, y, intervals).
71	
72	    `feature_fn` is pluggable so a WASB substrate can supply shuttle-motion
73	    features without changing the labeling logic. Returns the feature matrix `X`,
74	    binary labels `y`, and the candidate intervals (handy for downstream merging).
75	    """
76	    intervals: List[Interval] = [
77	        (float(r["start_time"]), float(r["end_time"])) for r in candidate_rows
78	    ]
79	    y = label_candidates(intervals, gt_intervals, iou_threshold)
80	    X = [feature_fn(r) for r in candidate_rows]
81	    return X, y, intervals
82	
[/external_agent_tool_result]
