[external_agent_tool_result]
1	"""A tiny, dependency-free logistic-regression classifier (numpy only).
2	
3	The offline learned segmenter (ADR-004) must run locally with no external API and
4	no heavy ML stack — the project already depends on numpy, and sklearn is not
5	installed. This implements just enough: standardized features, balanced-class
6	gradient descent, sigmoid probabilities, and JSON (de)serialization so a trained
7	model is a small, human-readable, version-controllable file.
8	
9	It deliberately stays simple: the rally-window features are few and roughly
10	linearly separable (high player motion + sustained activity => real rally), so a
11	well-regularized linear model is a sensible, inspectable baseline. Swap in a
12	richer model later behind the same fit/predict_proba/save/load interface.
13	"""
14	
15	import json
16	from typing import List, Optional
17	
18	import numpy as np
19	
20	
21	class LogisticRegression:
22	    def __init__(self, lr: float = 0.1, epochs: int = 1000, l2: float = 1e-3):
23	        self.lr = lr
24	        self.epochs = epochs
25	        self.l2 = l2
26	        self.w: Optional[np.ndarray] = None
27	        self.b: float = 0.0
28	        self.mean: Optional[np.ndarray] = None
29	        self.std: Optional[np.ndarray] = None
30	        self.feature_names: Optional[List[str]] = None
31	
32	    @staticmethod
33	    def _sigmoid(z: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
34	        # Clip for numerical stability (avoid overflow in exp).
35	        return 1.0 / (1.0 + np.exp(-np.clip(z, -30, 30)))
36	
37	    def _standardize(self, X: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
38	        return (X - self.mean) / self.std
39	
40	    def fit(self, X, y, feature_names: Optional[List[str]] = None,
41	            class_weight: str = "balanced") -> "LogisticRegression":
42	        """Fit with optional balanced class weighting (rally windows are imbalanced
43	        — many spurious candidates per real rally)."""
44	        X = np.asarray(X, dtype=float)
45	        y = np.asarray(y, dtype=float)
46	        if X.ndim != 2 or X.shape[0] == 0:
47	            raise ValueError("X must be a non-empty 2D array")
48	        self.feature_names = feature_names
49	
50	        self.mean = X.mean(axis=0)
51	        self.std = X.std(axis=0)
52	        self.std[self.std == 0] = 1.0  # guard constant features
53	        Xs = self._standardize(X)
54	        n, d = Xs.shape
55	
56	        if class_weight == "balanced":
57	            n_pos = max(1, int(y.sum()))
58	            n_neg = max(1, int((1 - y).sum()))
59	            w_pos, w_neg = n / (2.0 * n_pos), n / (2.0 * n_neg)
60	        else:
61	            w_pos = w_neg = 1.0
62	        sw = np.where(y == 1, w_pos, w_neg)
63	
64	        self.w = np.zeros(d)
65	        self.b = 0.0
66	        for _ in range(self.epochs):
67	            p = self._sigmoid(Xs @ self.w + self.b)
68	            err = (p - y) * sw
69	            grad_w = Xs.T @ err / n + self.l2 * self.w
70	            grad_b = float(err.mean())
71	            self.w -= self.lr * grad_w
72	            self.b -= self.lr * grad_b
73	        return self
74	
75	    def predict_proba(self, X) -> np.ndarray:
76	        if self.w is None:
77	            raise RuntimeError("model is not fitted")
78	        Xs = self._standardize(np.asarray(X, dtype=float))
79	        return self._sigmoid(Xs @ self.w + self.b)
80	
81	    def predict(self, X, threshold: float = 0.5) -> np.ndarray:
82	        return (self.predict_proba(X) >= threshold).astype(int)
83	
84	    # --- persistence (small JSON, no pickle/joblib) ---------------------------
85	
86	    def to_dict(self) -> dict:
87	        return {
88	            "model": "logreg",
89	            "lr": self.lr, "epochs": self.epochs, "l2": self.l2,
90	            "w": self.w.tolist() if self.w is not None else None,
91	            "b": self.b,
92	            "mean": self.mean.tolist() if self.mean is not None else None,
93	            "std": self.std.tolist() if self.std is not None else None,
94	            "feature_names": self....

### 645. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.977Z)

Honest result — and it's important, so I'll give it to you straight rather than dress it up.

**The distilled model is built and evaluated** (LogisticRegression on 5 resolution/fps-invariant features, trained on Gemini labels, honest leave-one-video-out). But on **2 videos it currently *underperforms* the dumb threshold baseline:**

| held-out | LEARNED F1 | baseline F1 |
|---|---|---|
| TestLargeVideo | 0.308 | 0.333 |
| Adarsh | 0.410 | 0.543 |
| **mean** | **0.359** | **0.438** |

Two root causes, both data/recall — **not** the method:
1. **n = 2 is too few.** With one training video, the classifier learns that video's feature distribution and it doesn't transfer to a different camera/resolution/style. Standardizing on a single video is unreliable.
2. **WASB proposal-recall ceiling.** Even my deliberately-permissive proposal only overlaps **3 of 7** Gemini rallies on TestLargeVideo (ceiling **0.43**) — WASB's windows just don't align with the rallies there. No classifier can exceed that. (Adarsh ceiling is 0.81, much healthier.)

So: the distilled-model *machinery* you wanted is done and ready, but it's **starving for data** — exactly the gap next week's videos fill. Right now the honest "best local config" is still the baseline windowing. Let me commit this (the framework is the durable asset), then talk about VeryLargeTestVideo.

Verified the schema. **You're exactly right** — and this is a better path than relying on Gemini's silver labels. Here's the precise flow.

**You as a rater → golden data → feeds the model:**
1. Make a CSV with the columns `rally_number,start_time,end_time` (start/end in seconds; it also accepts JSON, lowercases headers, and takes optional `shots_count`/`ending_reason`):
   ```
   rally_number,start_time,end_time
   1,8.8,11.5
   2,24.4,46.6
   ```
2. Register it as curated golden data (it **validates** the rallies first):
   ```
   python -m src.cli.curate import-local --sport badminton \
     --video-path "<video>" --annotations-path "<your_rallies.csv>" --license Proprietary
   ```
3. `python -m src.cli.curate export` emits it in the indexer's eval format → drop it into the calibration manifest → `calibrate_local` / `distill_local` pick it up.

Why this matters: your hand-rated timestamps are **human golden labels**, which are *better* than the Gemini silver labels the distillation has been training on. So your becoming a rater directly attacks the two things that capped the model — **too few labeled videos** and **label quality**. Each video you rate is one more (high-quality) training+eval point.

<result>{"question":"Research alternative methods to automatically detect rally start/end segments (temporal segmentation) in racket-sport video — especially badminton — for a SELF-HOSTED, LOCAL-FIRST pipeline, and assess whether a cloud-VLM teacher → local-model distillation is the best approach or whether other methods are competitive or complementary. Keep it focused (\"mini\" research): prioritize actionable, commercially-licensable, locally-runnable approaches over exotic SOTA needing huge compute or restrictive licenses.\n\nOUR CONTEXT / CONSTRAINTS (ground every recommendation in these):\n- Self-hosted on a single consumer GPU (RTX 2070 Super), Windows + WSL2. We want LOCAL-ONLY inference at runtime — privacy lever: user video never leaves the device. It's a commercial product, so PERMISSIVE / commercial-friendly licenses are REQUIRED (avoid AGPL and non-commercial-only weights/datasets).\n- Current substrate: WASB (MIT) shuttle-trajectory detector → per-frame (x, y, visibility). We derive rally windows via velocity/merge-gap/min-duration thresholds plus a net-crossing gate. We ALSO have a permissive person detector available (torchvision Faster R-CNN + supervision ByteTrack) and the video's AUDIO track.\n- Current best approach: use paid Gemini (cloud VLM, full-context) OFFLINE as a TEACHER to label rallies, then DISTILL into a local model (logistic regression on shuttle-trajectory features). Findings so far: threshold-tuning ceilings around F1 ≈ 0.44 and overfits with few videos; the learned model is data-starved at n=2 labeled videos; WASB candidate RECALL is a real bottleneck (its windows sometimes don't align with true rallies at IoU 0.5).\n- Inputs we can exploit locally: video frames, the shuttle trajectory, player bounding boxes / pose (if we add a permissive pose model), and the AUDIO track (shuttle \"thwack\" hits, crowd noise, umpire/score calls).\n\nSPECIFICALLY COVER:\n1. Method families for rally/point segmentation in racket sports (badminton, tennis, table tennis, pickleball): audio-based shot/hit detection; temporal action localization (TAL) deep models; pose/player-stance + court-homography state machines (e.g., \"serve-ready stance\" gating); classical change-point detection / HMM on the shuttle trajectory; scoreboard / score-overlay OCR for point boundaries; self-supervised / weakly-supervised approaches; and VLM teacher→student distillation.\n2. For EACH family: how it works, the labels/data it needs, expected accuracy, local-inference COMPUTE cost, and LICENSE / commercial viability of any models, datasets, or weights involved.\n3. Badminton rally-segmentation datasets (e.g., ShuttleSet, TrackNet/TrackNetV2 data) and their licenses / commercial usability.\n4. A RANKED recommendation for our local-first, permissive, single-GPU setup: is Gemini-teacher → local-distillation the best, or are some alternatives competitive or complementary — especially AUDIO-based hit detection and pose/court-geometry state machines, which we can run fully locally?\n5. Concrete, cheap next experiments we could run with what we already have (shuttle trajectory + person detector + audio).","summary":"For a local-first, single-GPU, commercially-licensed badminton rally-segmentation pipeline, the evidence does NOT crown the Gemini-teacher → local-distillation approach as uniquely best; instead it points to a complementary, multi-cue local stack as the strongest direction. Three fully-local method families are each independently validated in racket sports: (a) audio hit detection (sub-millisecond impact timing; classical MFCC+GMM and lightweight CNNs run on CPU), (b) pose/court-geometry state machines for serve-ready START + shuttle-flight-gradient END (reported ~95% on the component steps), and (c) compact recurrent hit detectors (MonoTrack HitNet, ~16K params) that consume exactly the inputs we already have (shuttle trajectory + pose + court corners). The literature also confirms that audio-visual fusion measurably improves rally classification over either modality alone, that VLM/MLLM teachers are best used as training-time priors rather than runtime localizers (so our teacher→student framing is sound but not the only competitive path), and that SSL+KD distillation is a proven top-tier strategy. The single most actionable near-term lever, given our current substrate and the WASB recall bottleneck, is to add a fully-local AUDIO hit-detection cue and fuse it with the shuttle trajectory; pose/court-geometry serve-gating is the strongest second add. A major dataset caveat: ShuttleSet and the broadcast-CNN approaches are derived from BWF broadcast footage with replays/score graphics/director cuts, and their high reported numbers are per-frame classification, not IoU boundary accuracy — they will not transfer cleanly to single-fixed-camera amateur video.","findings":[{"claim":"Pose + court-geometry state machines are a viable FULLY-LOCAL alternative for rally boundaries: serve-ready/serving stance (court-detail + pose features, classified by BiLSTM/CNN) marks rally START, and shuttlecock-flight gradient analysis marks rally END. ### 709. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.981Z)

[external_agent_tool_result]
=== SUMMARY ===
 For a local-first, single-GPU, commercially-licensed badminton rally-segmentation pipeline, the evidence does NOT crown the Gemini-teacher → local-distillation approach as uniquely best; instead it points to a complementary, multi-cue local stack as the strongest direction. Three fully-local method families are each independently validated in racket sports: (a) audio hit detection (sub-millisecond impact timing; classical MFCC+GMM and lightweight CNNs run on CPU), (b) pose/court-geometry state machines for serve-ready START + shuttle-flight-gradient END (reported ~95% on the component steps), and (c) compact recurrent hit detectors (MonoTrack HitNet, ~16K params) that consume exactly the inputs we already have (shuttle trajectory + pose + court corners). The literature also confirms that audio-visual fusion measurably improves rally classification over either modality alone, that VLM/MLLM teachers are best used as training-time priors rather than runtime localizers (so our teacher→student framing is sound but not the only competitive path), and that SSL+KD distillation is a proven top-tier strategy. The single most actionable near-term lever, given our current substrate and the WASB recall bottleneck, is to add a fully-local AUDIO hit-detection cue and fuse it with the shuttle trajectory; pose/court-geometry serve-gating is the strongest second add. A major dataset caveat: ShuttleSet and the broadcast-CNN approaches are derived from BWF broadcast footage with replays/score graphics/director cuts, and their high reported numbers are per-frame classification, not IoU boundary accuracy — they will not transfer cleanly to single-fixed-camera amateur video.

=== 12 VERIFIED FINDINGS ===

[1] conf=high vote=2-1 and 3-0
CLAIM: Pose + court-geometry state machines are a viable FULLY-LOCAL alternative for rally boundaries: serve-ready/serving stance (court-detail + pose features, classified by BiLSTM/CNN) marks rally START, and shuttlecock-flight gradient analysis marks rally END. Inputs are hand-engineered 12-frame x 26-feature (distance/angle/gradient/movement) matrices, not raw frames — i.e., cheap to run locally with no large model.
SRC: https://link.springer.com/chapter/10.1007/978-3-031-69073-0_15; https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s42979-025-03935-0

[2] conf=high vote=3-0
CLAIM: A compact recurrent hit detector (MonoTrack's HitNet) detects badminton hit events — which segment a rally into successive strokes — using a ~16K-parameter 2-layer GRU over a 12-frame sliding window of court corners + both players' poses + shuttle location. These are exactly the locally-available, permissive inputs our pipeline already has (shuttle trajectory + person/pose + court geometry), and the model is trivially runnable on our GPU.
SRC: https://ar5iv.labs.arxiv.org/html/2204.01899

[3] conf=high vote=3-0
CLAIM: Audio-based hit detection is a high-resolution, fully-local cue for racket-sport hit timing. A two-stage energy-peak + small-CNN pipeline detects ball-racket contacts with sub-millisecond timing (peaks within ~0.2 ms even with noise; peak-detection precision 0.98, recall 0.95-1.0), and a classical MFCC+GMM pipeline (39-D MFCC, per-class GMMs, likelihood-ratio confidence) is CPU-light and applicable to detecting shuttle 'thwack' hits.
SRC: https://arxiv.org/html/2409.11760v1; https://www.researchgate.net/publication/261119682_Detection_of_ball_hits_in_a_tennis_game_using_audio_and_visual_information

[4] conf=high vote=3-0 and 2-1
CLAIM: Audio-only hit detection is only as good as its post-processing: naive raw-frame probabilities give a poor F-score (~41-50), but adding a simple likelihood-ratio confidence measure raises audio-only F-score to ~68-77 by cutting false positives. A standalone XGBoost impact classifier hits 0.98 median accuracy on isolated samples, but the full on-court system drops to ~85% — real-world deployment degrades audio-only hit detection substantially.
SRC: https://www.r...

### 715. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.981Z)

## Research posted — rally-detection methods

Full cited report saved to [docs/RALLY_DETECTION_RESEARCH.md](docs/RALLY_DETECTION_RESEARCH.md) (23 sources, 110 claims → 25 adversarially verified, 6 refuted). The headline:

**Verdict: teacher→student is sound but *not* uniquely best.** The literature actually validates our exact framing (VLM as an *offline training-time prior*, not a runtime localizer) — but it says the strongest direction is to **augment** it with a complementary **fully-local multi-cue stack**, not rely on distillation alone.

**Three local, permissive, competitive cues — ranked by leverage for us:**
1. **🔊 Audio hit detection (shuttle "thwack") — the #1 cheap lever.** We're ignoring the audio track entirely, and it's a high-resolution rally signal. The killer evidence: in a tennis system, audio+visual **fusion lifted rally recall to 83% vs 54% visual-only** — and *recall* is exactly our WASB bottleneck. CPU-light (MFCC+GMM or energy-peak+small-CNN). Caveat: needs likelihood-ratio post-processing (raw peaks are weak, ~41–50 F → ~68–77 F).
2. **🧍 Pose/court serve-gate** — this is *literally our "gate B" idea, validated*: serve-ready stance → START, shuttle-flight-gradient → END. We can prototype a cheap version with the person detector we already have (no pose model).
3. **🧠 Compact HitNet GRU** (~16K params, MonoTrack) on pose+trajectory+court — a learned stroke primitive.

**Honest caveats it surfaced (important):**
- Almost every flashy number (95–99%) is from **broadcast** footage with replays/score-graphics and is **per-frame accuracy, not IoU boundary F1** — it will **not** transfer cleanly to your single-camera amateur clips. (The "95% beats your 0.44" claim was explicitly *refuted* as a metric mismatch.)
- **License gap (hard req):** ShuttleSet, MonoTrack/HitNet weights, and even the WASB *weights* commercial terms are unconfirmed — must verify before commercial use.

**Cheapest next experiments (zero/low new training, all in the doc):** E1 — extract audio, energy-peak the impacts, overlay hit-density on WASB windows and measure whether audio *recovers the rallies WASB misses* (directly tests the recall bottleneck). E3 — add audio features (hit-rate, time-since-last-hit, applause-energy) to the distillation student and re-eval at IoU 0.5.

Net: when we resume, **adding audio is the highest-ROI move**, and your **pose serve-gate instinct was right**. Both run fully local.

The **VLC annotator** workflow (`w6nwcpw33`) is still building — I'll post the plugin + install steps when it lands, then we're parked for your rating. (docs/RALLY_DETECTION_RESEARCH.md is uncommitted on master — say the word and I'll commit it.)

### 725. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.982Z)

[external_agent_tool_result]
1	# Badminton Rally Annotator (VLC extension) -- install & usage
2	
3	## 1. Install (Windows)
4	1. Create the folder if it does not exist:
5	   `C:\Users\<you>\AppData\Roaming\vlc\lua\extensions\`
6	   (i.e. `%APPDATA%\vlc\lua\extensions\`).
7	2. Copy `rally_annotator.lua` into that folder.
8	3. In VLC: **Tools -> Plugins and extensions -> Reload extensions**
9	   (or just restart VLC). Requires VLC 3.0.x (tested target 3.0.x).
10	4. Enable it from the **View** menu -> **Badminton Rally Annotator**.
11	   The dialog appears (it stays open while you watch).
12	
13	> Tip: if you run the `timeosd` interface
14	> (`%APPDATA%\vlc\lua\intf\timeosd.lua`) you also get a live on-screen clock
15	> while you mark -- the two work together.
16	
17	## 2. Rate workflow
18	1. Open the badminton video in VLC and start playback. Pause/scrub freely.
19	2. When a rally begins, click **Mark START** (snapshots the current time).
20	3. Pick the point-stop reason in the **Ending reason** dropdown
21	   (winner / forced_error / unforced_error / service_fault / let / other).
22	4. When the rally ends, click **Mark END** -- one CSV row is appended
23	   immediately. The dropdown value at that moment is what gets written.
24	5. Made a mistake? **Undo last** disarms an uncommitted START, or removes the
25	   last written row (one level of undo).
26	6. The status panel shows: last action, current time, whether a START is armed,
27	   rallies written, and the **CSV output path**.
28	
29	**Output path:** written next to the video as `<video-stem>.rallies.csv`
30	when VLC can resolve the file path; otherwise it falls back to your home dir
31	(`%USERPROFILE%\rally_labels.csv`). The exact path is always shown in the dialog.
32	
33	**CSV format:** header `rally_number,start_time,end_time,ending_reason`,
34	times in **decimal seconds**. Re-enabling the extension keeps appending to the
35	same file (it does not truncate) and **continues** rally numbering from the
36	highest number already in the file, so you never get duplicate `rally_number`s.
37	
38	## 3. Ingest with the in-repo loaders
39	The CSV is consumed unchanged by this repo's golden-set tooling, which reads
40	`start_time`/`end_time` (decimal seconds) via `csv.DictReader` and drops any
41	row with `end <= start`:
42	
43	```
44	# Per-video WASB calibration against your labels:
45	python -m backend.eval.calibrate_wasb --trajectory full_traj.csv \
46	  --labels "C:\path\to\match.rallies.csv" --fps 59.94 --frame-width 1920
47	
48	# Slice rally clips out of the match to eyeball boundaries:
49	python -m backend.tools.rally_slicer --video "C:\path\to\match.mp4" \
50	  --labels "C:\path\to\match.rallies.csv" --rallies all
51	```
52	
53	> **Other repo:** the separate **sports-data-collector** repo exposes an
54	> `import-local` command for the same CSV; that command does **not** live in
55	> this repository, so run it from the collector checkout:
56	> `python -m src.cli.curate import-local --sport badminton --video-path ... --annotations-path match.rallies.csv`.
57	
58	## 4. Notes & gotchas
59	- VLC's `time` is in MICROSECONDS; the extension divides by 1e6 -> seconds.
60	- It does not auto-react to pause on purpose (that callback is broken in VLC 4.0
61	  and flaky on macOS). You explicitly click START/END, which is more precise.
62	- Keep `ending_reason` to the 6 dropdown values -- downstream QA enforces the
63	  closed vocabulary; the loaders carry it verbatim and do not validate it.
64	- A rally must be > 0s: if END is at/before START it is either rejected (equal)
65	  or swapped to a positive interval (END earlier than START), keeping the file
66	  free of degenerate rows the loaders would drop anyway.
67	- The status panel is a rich-text (HTML) widget, so multi-line output renders
68	  reliably across VLC 3.x Qt builds.
69	
[/external_agent_tool_result]

**Built, verified (high confidence), and installed.** Restart VLC (or **Tools → Plugins and extensions → Reload extensions**), then enable it from the **View menu → "Badminton Rally Annotator"**.

**Rate workflow:**
1. Open a badminton video, play/scrub freely.
2. At a rally's first serve → **Mark START** (snapshots the exact time).
3. Pick the **Ending reason** in the dropdown (winner / forced_error / unforced_error / service_fault / let / other).
4. At rally end → **Mark END** → one row is appended instantly. **Undo last** fixes mistakes (one level).

**Output:** `<video-stem>.rallies.csv` next to the video, columns `rally_number,start_time,end_time,ending_reason` in decimal seconds. Re-enabling keeps appending and **continues numbering** (no duplicate IDs — that was a bug the verify pass caught and fixed). The dialog always shows the live path + rally count.

**It ingests unchanged** into:
- `python -m backend.eval.calibrate_wasb --labels <your>.rallies.csv ...` (calibration/distillation)
- `python -m backend.tools.rally_slicer --labels <your>.rallies.csv --rallies all` (eyeball clips)

1	# Architecture Decision Records
2	
3	## ADR-001: Precise shuttle tracking via self-hosted TrackNetV4/WASB on WSL2
4	
5	**Date:** 2026-05-25
6	**Status:** Accepted
7	
8	### Context
9	
10	The pipeline uses an AI multimodal provider (Gemini) for *semantic* rally segmentation.
11	We need **precise frame-level detection of the moving shuttlecock** (and, generically,
12	balls for other sports) to produce higher-quality candidate rallies and a fine-tuning
13	signal.
14	
15	We evaluated whether a commercially available model/API could do this. Findings:
16	
17	- **Multimodal LLM APIs (Gemini, Claude, GPT-class) are the wrong tool for precise
18	  localization.** They reason well about *what* is happening (rally vs. dead time) but
19	  cannot reliably track a tiny, fast (~up to 400 km/h), motion-blurred shuttle frame by
20	  frame.
21	- The precision leaders are **purpose-built trajectory models**, which are open source and
22	  self-hosted, not big-vendor APIs:
23	  - **TrackNetV4** (TensorFlow) — badminton-specific, heatmap + motion-attention,
24	    highest reported shuttle accuracy; ships `src/predict.py` producing per-frame (X, Y)
25	    coordinate CSV. https://github.com/AR4152/TrackNetV4
26	  - **WASB** (PyTorch) — multi-sport strong baseline (badminton/tennis/volleyball/…),
27	    fits our `SportProfile` abstraction; needs a custom-video inference wrapper.
28	    https://github.com/nttcom/WASB-SBDT
29	- True turnkey commercial APIs (Roboflow hosted models) exist but cap below a tuned
30	  TrackNet; enterprise systems (Hawk-Eye, SkillCorner, TRACAB) are not API-accessible.
31	
32	### Decision
33	
34	1. Adopt **self-hosted TrackNetV4** as the primary precise shuttle detector, with **WASB**
35	   as the multi-sport fallback.
36	2. Run these models under **WSL2 (Ubuntu)**, **not** native Ubuntu. The entire indexer
37	   already runs on Windows; WSL2 keeps a single dev environment and quarantines the
38	   TensorFlow/PyTorch dependencies. Native Ubuntu would require dual-boot and migrating
39	   the whole project for marginal gain.
40	3. Integrate via a **subprocess adapter**: a detector module that shells out to the WSL
41	   `predict.py`, reads the coordinate CSV, and converts trajectory → candidate rallies
42	   feeding the existing stitching flow. This keeps the TF dependency fully isolated from
43	   the main Python environment.
44	
45	### Consequences
46	
47	- **WSL I/O caveat:** `/mnt/c` access is slow. Stage match videos inside the WSL
48	  filesystem (e.g. `~/clips/`) before inference rather than reading large mp4s across the
49	  Windows↔WSL boundary.
50	- TrackNetV4 (TensorFlow) and WASB (PyTorch) live in **separate conda envs**.
51	- Next step after integration: manual video annotation → fine-tune the detector.
52	
53	---
54	
55	## ADR-002: Use the sports-data-collector's rally annotations as a golden eval + temporal-segmenter training set
56	
57	**Date:** 2026-05-26
58	**Status:** Accepted
59	
60	### Context
61	
62	A sibling project, **sports-data-collector**, curates sports videos and stores per-rally
63	`[start, end)` timestamps (plus stroke counts, scores) in an SQLite DB, with a
64	commercial-license flag per source. We asked whether this data could improve the
65	indexer's rally detection, and for which task.
66	
67	Key findings:
68	
69	- The annotations are **temporal-only** (when rallies start/end). They do **not** contain
70	  per-frame shuttle `(x, y)` coordinates, so they **cannot train the shuttle detector**
71	  (TrackNetV4/WASB — see ADR-001), which needs coordinate labels.
72	- They are an excellent **golden evaluation set** and a **training signal for the
73	  *temporal* rally segmenter** (active vs. dead play over time) — the stage that is today
74	  a hand-tuned heuristic, not a learned model.
75	- The collector's five sports tables (`badminton_rallies`, `tennis_points`,
76	  `cricket_deliveries`, `pickleball_rallies`, `tabletennis_rallies`) all share a
77	  `(video_id, ordinal, start_time, end_tim...

1	# Video Recording Guidelines (DRAFT — thresholds pending empirical data)
2	
3	**Status:** 🚧 Draft / placeholder · **Date:** 2026-05-30
4	**Why this exists:** highlight quality is bounded by *input* quality. To get reliably good auto-generated highlights, the source video must be recorded a certain way (camera height, court position, resolution, frame rate, stability, framing). We want to (1) **derive** the minimum acceptable capture spec **empirically** from the precision/recall of the solutions we build, and (2) **publish user-facing best practices** so a user knows how to record footage that the product can index well.
5	
6	> **This is a tracked thought, not a finished standard.** The exact numeric thresholds below are **placeholders** to be replaced with values measured against our golden set. See "How we'll set the real numbers" (§3). Captured now (per the team's working agreement) so it isn't lost; to be revisited.
7	>
8	> **Update (2026-06-02):** the **frame-rate** and **camera-geometry** rows now carry **literature-backed starting values** (cited inline). They remain *starting points* to confirm empirically per §3. The golden-set **corpus** diversity spec (what footage to collect, not just how users should record) is now in [GOLDEN_SET_PHASE1_VIDEOS.md](GOLDEN_SET_PHASE1_VIDEOS.md).
9	
10	---
11	
12	## 1. Why input capture matters (grounded in what we already know)
13	
14	ADR-004 found a concrete, capture-dependent failure mode: on **broadcast** footage the camera *tracks the players*, so player pixel-motion dips during rallies and spikes on pans/replays — making player-motion a poor rally proxy. The lesson generalizes: **the pipeline's accuracy is conditional on how the video was shot.** A **static, full-court** camera (typical of a fixed tripod recording) behaves very differently from broadcast — and is likely much friendlier to both the motion/detector stages and the shuttle-tracking substrate (WASB/TrackNet, ADR-001).
15	
16	So capture guidance isn't cosmetic — it directly moves precision/recall.
17	
18	---
19	
20	## 2. The dimensions that likely matter (hypotheses to test)
21	
22	| Dimension | Why it plausibly affects P/R | Placeholder guidance (UNVERIFIED) |
23	|---|---|---|
24	| **Resolution** | Shuttle is tiny & fast; too few pixels → detector misses it | ≥ **1080p** preferred; **720p** likely floor; 2K/4K may help shuttle tracking but ↑ compute/cost |
25	| **Frame rate** | Motion blur; boundary precision; fast-ball detection | ≥ **30 fps** badminton/pickleball; **≥ 60 fps table tennis** — 30 fps collapses fast-ball detection (**22.5% → 93.2%** at 60 fps; 120 ≈ 60, [Nature Sci Rep](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41598-023-42966-6)) |
26	| **Camera position** | Static full-court view avoids the broadcast camera-tracking trap (ADR-004) | **Fixed tripod**, elevated, behind/above one baseline, whole court in frame |
27	| **Camera height & angle** | Occlusion of players/shuttle; court keystoning | **3–5 m** elevated (~4.5 m), **~30° down-tilt**, behind one baseline, whole court + net headroom ([badminton-CV setup](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10747833/)) |
28	| **Framing / margins** | Cropping the court or players breaks detection & relevance check | Entire court + a margin; players never leave frame |
29	| **Stability** | Handheld jitter inflates motion signals | Tripod / fixed mount; no panning or zoom during play |
30	| **Lighting / exposure** | Blur & contrast affect detection and the existing blur/relevance validators | Even indoor lighting; avoid backlight/glare; minimize motion blur |
31	| **Court contrast / background** | Relevance check uses court color; busy crowds add false motion | Clear court markings; minimal moving background where possible |
32	| **Codec / container** | Pipeline already validates MP4 + decodes H.264/HEVC | MP4 (H.264/HEVC) as today; align with MONETIZATION_AUDIT.md codec notes |
33	
34	These map onto checks that **already exist** in `backend/validators/` (resolution...
[/external_agent_tool_result]

### 763. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.984Z)

## 🔊 The audio plan — committed & documented (PR #32)

Everything's checked in: **ADR-007** (the decision), **`docs/AUDIO_RALLY_DETECTION_PLAN.md`** (the detailed plan), the **research report**, the **"audio always on" recording requirement**, and the **VLC annotator**. Here's the plan in plain terms.

**Core idea:** a rally is a *cluster of hits with rhythmic cadence*; dead time has no hits; a **single isolated "thwack" = the between-point service feed** (exactly our false-fire). So the audio doesn't replace WASB — it **fuses** with it, and its biggest job is **recall**: propose a rally wherever there's a hit-cluster but WASB found nothing (the gap that ceilinged us at ~0.43).

**Pipeline (all local, CPU-light, permissive — numpy+scipy):**
1. `ffmpeg` → mono WAV.
2. Onset/energy-peak detection (no training) → candidate impact timestamps.
3. Confidence/false-positive suppression (likelihood-ratio + a tiny MFCC classifier) — the research showed raw peaks are weak (~41–50 F) but post-processing lifts them to ~68–77.
4. Cluster hits → rally: **START ≈ first hit (serve), END ≈ last hit + tail**; drop lone hits.
5. Fuse with WASB: recall-recovery + audio features (`hit_count`, `hit_rate`, cadence regularity, `time_since_last_hit`) into the distilled student.

**On your GoPro-audio doubt — you're right to flag it, and it's the #1 risk.** A shuttle "thwack" is *much quieter* than a tennis/table-tennis impact, so SNR transfer is genuinely uncertain. That's why **E1 is an explicit GO/NO-GO probe on your real GoPro audio** before we build anything heavy: extract audio → peak-detect → overlay hit-density on a video you've rated → does it (a) align with real rallies and (b) recover the ones WASB misses? If E1 fails, we defer audio honestly rather than sink effort. Making **"record with audio on, mic unobstructed"** a camera-setup requirement (now in the guidelines) is the right move — it's a cheap insurance that protects the cue, and yes, please add it to your camera-setup doc.

**Sequencing locked:** **audio now → [your other hunch — slot reserved] → pose/stance (#2, parked, the harder one).**

**What unblocks E1:** one or two videos rated with the **VLC annotator** (so I have golden rally boundaries to score against). The moment you've rated a clip, I run E1 and we get a real answer on shuttle-audio efficacy.

When you're ready to share that hunch, I'm all ears — and whenever a rated CSV exists, ping me and I'll kick off E1. Open PR to merge: **#32**.

3	  Mark badminton rally START / END + a point-stop reason while watching, and
4	  append one CSV row per rally that this repo's golden-set loaders (and the
5	  separate sports-data-collector `import-local` command) ingest directly.
6	
7	  Output CSV columns (decimal SECONDS):
8	      rally_number,start_time,end_time,ending_reason
9	
10	  WHY a button-dialog (not an auto-pause hook):
11	    VLC 3.x can expose pause via the {"playing-listener"} capability
12	    (status_changed/playing_changed), but that callback is flaky on macOS
13	    (VLC #22778) and the input/meta listeners are broken in VLC 4.0 (#27558).
14	    A persistent dialog whose button callbacks SNAPSHOT the current playback
15	    time is portable, precise, and lets the rater pause/scrub freely before
16	    committing a mark. So we use NO listener capabilities.
17	
18	  UNITS: in VLC 3.x, vlc.var.get(input,"time") is in MICROSECONDS -> /1e6.
19	
20	  Install (Windows): copy this file to
21	      %APPDATA%\vlc\lua\extensions\rally_annotator.lua
22	  then VLC > Tools > Plugins and extensions > Reload extensions (or restart),
23	  then enable it from the View menu.
24	]]

32	    version     = "1.1",

64	-- Current playback time in SECONDS, or nil if nothing is playing.
65	local function now_seconds()
66	  local input = vlc.object.input()
67	  if not input then return nil end
68	  local t_us = vlc.var.get(input, "time")   -- MICROSECONDS in VLC 3.x
69	  if not t_us then return nil end
70	  return t_us / 1000000.0
71	end
72	
73	-- mm:ss.mmm for display only.
74	local function fmt_clock(s)
75	  if not s then return "--:--" end
76	  if s < 0 then s = 0 end
77	  local m   = math.floor(s / 60)
78	  local sec = s - m * 60
79	  return string.format("%d:%06.3f", m, sec)
80	end
81

84	  text = tostring(text or "")
85	  text = text:gsub("&", "&amp;")
86	  text = text:gsub("<", "&lt;")
87	  text = text:gsub(">", "&gt;")
88	  return text

### 813. user (2026-07-02T06:11:32.987Z)

For the audio plumbing, I am trying to understand that whether we will integrate it in a way that we get audio as a separate signal for the entire video and then in code we try to fuse with WASB and make corrections to WASB models output, or we are going to feed the audio to WASB in a way that it uses audio signals to further refine its metrics which help us detect rallies better?

### 814. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.987Z)

Great question — it's the key architectural fork, and the answer is firmly **Option A: audio is a separate whole-video signal, fused in *our* rally layer — we do not feed audio into WASB.** Here's the why, because it's grounded in what WASB actually is.

**WASB is a vision-only shuttle *detector*, not a rally detector.** It's a frozen, pretrained CNN (HRNet) that takes *image frames* → per-frame shuttle `(x, y, visibility)`. Two consequences:
- It has **no audio pathway**. Making it ingest audio means adding an audio encoder + fusion layers and **retraining the whole network** — which (a) needs **per-frame shuttle-coordinate labels paired with audio**, which we don't have (we only have rally `[start,end]` labels), and (b) forks the clean MIT model we rely on.
- **It doesn't decide rallies at all.** Rally detection is *our* layer on top of WASB's trajectory (`trajectory_to_action_windows` + gate + the distilled classifier). So "audio helping detect rallies better" already happens in our code — the only question is whether audio enters WASB (no) or our rally layer (yes).

And conceptually, audio answers a *different* question than WASB: WASB says **where** the shuttle is on screen; audio says **when** a hit happened. Audio doesn't make WASB localize the shuttle better — it's an independent rally-activity cue. So fusing them belongs at the layer that *combines cues into rallies*, not inside the shuttle detector.

**So the data flow is:**
```
frames ─► WASB (frozen, MIT) ─► shuttle trajectory ─┐
                                                     ├─► RALLY LAYER (our code) ─► rallies
audio  ─► hit detector (our code) ─► hit events ────┘   (fusion happens HERE)
```

Within that rally layer, "fusion" has **two complementary modes** (both in the plan):
1. **Feature-level fusion (primary — refines our rally metrics):** add audio features (`hit_rate`, inter-hit cadence/regularity, `time_since_last_hit`) alongside WASB-derived features (velocity, net-crossings) into the **one distilled classifier** that emits rallies. This is experiment **E3** — it's literally "use audio to make our rally metrics better."
2. **Decision-level recall-recovery (corrects WASB's output):** where WASB proposed nothing but there's a dense hit-cluster, propose a rally there. This is the "make corrections to WASB output" use — it directly attacks the recall bottleneck.

This also keeps the architecture **modular**: audio is a *peer cue* sitting beside the shuttle trajectory (and, later, pose), and WASB stays a swappable black box (we could drop in TrackNet without touching the audio path).

**The one scenario that resembles your Option B** isn't WASB at all — it's a *future* single end-to-end multimodal model that eats frames+audio → rallies directly (the "HitNet GRU / temporal-action-localization" direction from the research). That's a bigger build needing far more labels, and it'd *replace* the modular stack rather than augment WASB. Not now; noted as a long-horizon option.

The plan doc says "fuse with WASB" but doesn't spell out *that WASB stays frozen and fusion is strictly in the rally layer* — which is exactly the confusion you just flagged. Want me to add a short **"Integration architecture"** section to `AUDIO_RALLY_DETECTION_PLAN.md` (with that diagram + the "WASB is not modified" principle) so it's unambiguous for whoever builds it?

### 867. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.990Z)

Captured exactly as you framed it. Here's what went into ADR-007 (new **"Integration architecture"** section) + the plan doc, in PR #34:

**Option A (now) — the detailed "fuse" explanation:**
- *Why not feed audio into WASB:* WASB is a **frozen, vision-only shuttle detector** (frames → x,y,visibility; MIT weights we run, not train). It has no audio path, doesn't decide rallies at all, and bolting audio on would mean **retraining** with per-frame shuttle+audio labels we don't have (forking the MIT model). And audio answers a *different* question — **"when" a hit happened**, not **"where"** the shuttle is.
- *So fusion lives in our rally layer,* in two modes: **feature-level** (audio features → the single distilled classifier, E3) and **decision-level recall-recovery** (a hit-cluster where WASB found nothing → propose a rally). Each cue stays a swappable black box. Added a data-flow diagram.

**Option B (the intended destination) — your call, now documented:**
> Once a healthy golden corpus exists, **collapse the modular cues into a single OWNED end-to-end multimodal model** (frames + audio + pose/stance + … → rallies), **fine-tuned on our own data instead of depending on WASB's pretrained weights** — removing both the recall ceiling WASB's windows impose and the licensing question on its academic weights.

And the rationale you gave — that the modular cues aren't a detour: **"Option A is how we earn Option B."** Each cue (audio → stance heuristic → other signals) is cheap, debuggable, and *generates the very labels* the end-to-end model will need. We jump to B only when the data supports training it.

**To fully clean the slate, two small docs/chore PRs to merge:**
- **#33** — removes the stale annotator copy that slipped onto master.
- **#34** — this ADR-007 expansion (+ repoints the annotator reference).

No conflicts between them. After both merge, master is pristine, the audio direction is fully specified, and Option B is on the record as the north star. 🧭

### 938. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.994Z)

Honest E1 read — this is **amber, not green**, and the numbers tell a clear story:

**The encouraging half:** audio fires in **100% of real rallies** on both videos, and **100% of the rallies WASB misses have audio hit-clusters** — so audio genuinely carries rally signal and *could* attack the recall bottleneck.

**The problem half:** the **discrimination ratio is weak (1.3–1.5×)** and the hit rate is high (**56–64 hits/min**) — the detector is firing almost as much in dead time as in rallies. So at k=2.5 it's catching lots of *non-shuttle* transients (footwork, voices, ambient), and audio-only segmentation is poor (F1 0.13–0.27, well below WASB's). That's the shuttle-SNR risk showing up.

Before I call the gate, let me check the key question: are real shuttle hits the *loudest* transients (so a stricter threshold cleanly isolates them)? A quick k-sweep on Adarsh:

### 939. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.994Z)

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: k-sweep: discrimination vs threshold on Adarsh
command: cd "C:/Users/avidu/Projects/badminton-highlight-indexer"
python - <<'PY'
from backend.audio.hit_detector import extract_audio, load_wav, detect_hits
from backend.eval.calibrate_wasb import load_golden_gts
from backend.utils.video import get_video_duration
import os
vid = r"C:/Users/avidu/Projects/Annotation Setup/Collect/Adarsh and Vasanth.MP4"
gts = load_golden_gts("output/adarsh_gemini_fullvideo.csv")
dur = get_video_duration(vid)
rally_dur = sum(e-s for s,e in gts)
dead_dur = dur - rally_dur
wav = extract_audio(vid); x, sr = load_wav(wav); os.remove(wav)
def hits_in(hits,s,e): return sum(1 for h in hits if s<=h.t<=e)
print(f"dur={dur:.0f}s rally={rally_dur:.0f}s dead={dead_dur:.0f}s | {len(gts)} rallies")
print(f"{'k':>4} {'hits':>6} {'hits/min':>9} {'in/s':>6} {'dead/s':>7} {'ratio':>6} {'rallies>=2hits':>14}")
for k in (2.5, 4, 6, 8, 12, 16):
    h = detect_hits(x, sr, k=k)
    hi = sum(hits_in(h,s,e) for s,e in gts)
    ho = len(h)-hi
    din = hi/max(1e-6,rally_dur); do = ho/max(1e-6,dead_dur)
    cov = sum(1 for s,e in gts if hits_in(h,s,e)>=2)
    print(f"{k:>4} {len(h):>6} {len(h)/dur*60:>9.1f} {din:>6.2f} {do:>7.2f} {din/do if do>0 else 99:>6.1f} {cov:>9}/{len(gts)}")
PY
[/external_agent_tool_call]

### 941. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.994Z)

**That's the key finding** — there's a clear sweet spot and a clear ceiling:

| k | hits/min | in-rally/s | dead-time/s | **discrimination** | rallies w/ ≥2 hits |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2.5 | 56 | 1.17 | 0.87 | 1.3× | 36/36 |
| **4** | **22** | **0.72** | **0.26** | **2.8×** | **30/36** |
| 6 | 0.6 | — | — | (collapses) | 0/36 |

So shuttle hits **are** somewhat louder than dead-time noise (discrimination climbs to **2.8×** at k=4 while still covering 30/36 rallies), but there's a cliff — and even at best it's only ~2.8×, not the 5–10× that would make audio clean on its own. ### 952. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.995Z)

Here's the honest E1 verdict, and where I'd go next.

## E1 verdict: AMBER — promising for *recall*, not clean enough to stand alone (yet)

**Built & shipped** (PR #35, 207 tests): the audio hit-detector + E1 probe — numpy/ffmpeg, no scipy, WASB untouched.

**The good (real signal):**
- Audio fires in **100% of rallies** on both videos.
- **100% of the rallies WASB *misses*** have audio hit-clusters → audio could directly attack our recall bottleneck.

**The caution (why it's amber):**
- **Discrimination is only ~2.8×** at best (in-rally vs dead-time hit density), with a sensitivity cliff just past k=4. At default sensitivity it's 1.3× and ~56 hits/min — the broadband detector is catching lots of *non-shuttle* transients (footwork, voices, ambient).
- **Audio-only F1 (0.13–0.27) trails WASB** → audio is a **fusion/recall cue, not a standalone segmenter**.

**Two honest caveats that make this a floor, not a ceiling:**
1. These are **Gemini *silver* labels** — the "dead time" likely contains unlabeled activity (warm-up hits, knock-ups), which *inflates* the dead-time hit count and *understates* the true discrimination. **Your human-gold labels will sharpen this.**
2. The detector is **v1 broadband** (pre-emphasis only). The obvious untried precision lever is a **shuttle-band band-pass** — a shuttle "click" is HF-rich; voices/footsteps are lower-frequency, so band-passing should separate them and lift discrimination well above 2.8×.

## The decision (your call)
- **(A) I add the shuttle-band band-pass now** — cheap, no labels needed, still pre-fusion. It directly tests "can we make the audio signal clean?" If discrimination jumps (say 4–6×), audio fusion becomes clearly worth building; if it doesn't budge, that's a strong honest signal to lean on stance instead. **My recommendation** — highest info per effort, and it parallelizes with your rating.
- **(B) Pause audio, wait for your human-gold labels**, then re-run E1 to get the true (un-penalized) discrimination before any more detector work.

I lean **(A)** — it's a quick, label-free experiment that either greenlights audio or tells us to pivot, while you keep rating. Want me to go? (And memory's already refreshed per the new auto-checkpoint policy — both `current-state` and the handoff reflect this E1 result.)

68	    hop_sec = hop / float(sr)
69	    if len(x) < frame:
70	        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float32), hop_sec
71	    win = np.hanning(frame).astype(np.float32)
72	    n_frames = 1 + (len(x) - frame) // hop
73	    idx = np.arange(frame)[None, :] + hop * np.arange(n_frames)[:, None]
74	    frames = x[idx] * win
75	    mag = np.abs(np.fft.rfft(frames, axis=1))
76	    diff = np.diff(mag, axis=0)                                  # frame-to-frame change
77	    flux = np.sqrt((np.maximum(diff, 0.0) ** 2).sum(axis=1))     # positive (onset) flux
78	    env = np.concatenate([[0.0], flux]).astype(np.float32)       # align to n_frames
79	    return env, hop_sec
80	
81	
82	def _moving_stats(env: np.ndarray, win: int) -> Tuple[np.ndarray, np.ndarray]:
83	    """Sliding-window mean and std (reflect-padded, via cumsum). win forced odd."""
84	    if win % 2 == 0:
85	        win += 1
86	    pad = win // 2
87	    p = np.pad(env.astype(np.float64), pad, mode="reflect")
88	    c1 = np.cumsum(np.insert(p, 0, 0.0))
89	    c2 = np.cumsum(np.insert(p * p, 0, 0.0))
90	    s1 = c1[win:] - c1[:-win]
91	    s2 = c2[win:] - c2[:-win]
92	    mean = s1 / win
93	    var = np.maximum(s2 / win - mean * mean, 0.0)

106	def detect_hits(samples: np.ndarray, sr: int, k: float = 2.5, win_frames: int = 41,
107	                min_gap_sec: float = 0.08) -> List[HitEvent]:
108	    """Detect impact transients: local maxima of the onset envelope that exceed an
109	    adaptive threshold (moving mean + k·std), keeping the strongest within `min_gap_sec`.
110	    `k` is the sensitivity knob (lower = more hits)."""
111	    env, hop_sec = onset_envelope(samples, sr)
112	    if len(env) < 3:
113	        return []
114	    mean, std = _moving_stats(env, win_frames)
115	    thr = mean + k * std
116	    is_peak = (env[1:-1] >= env[:-2]) & (env[1:-1] > env[2:]) & (env[1:-1] > thr[1:-1])
117	    peak_idx = np.nonzero(is_peak)[0] + 1
118	    if peak_idx.size == 0:
119	        return []

import os
from backend.audio.hit_detector import extract_audio, load_wav, detect_hits
from backend.eval.calibrate_wasb import load_golden_gts
from backend.utils.video import get_video_duration

vid = r"C:/Users/avidu/Projects/Annotation Setup/Collect/Adarsh and Vasanth.MP4"
gts = load_golden_gts("output/adarsh_gemini_fullvideo.csv")
dur = get_video_duration(vid)
rally_dur = sum(e-s for s,e in gts); dead_dur = dur - rally_dur
print(f"Adarsh: dur={dur:.0f}s rally={rally_dur:.0f}s dead={dead_dur:.0f}s | {len(gts)} rallies")
wav = extract_audio(vid, sr=32000); x, sr = load_wav(wav); os.remove(wav)
print(f"audio: sr={sr} samples={len(x)}")
def hits_in(h,s,e): return sum(1 for z in h if s<=z.t<=e)
bands = [None,(1000,4000),(2000,8000),(3000,9000),(4000,12000),(5000,15000),(6000,15000)]
print(f"\n{'band':>14} {'k':>4} {'hits/min':>8} {'in/s':>6} {'dead/s':>7} {'ratio':>6} {'cov>=2':>8}")
best=None

    for k in (2.5,3.5,5.0):
        h = detect_hits(x, sr, k=k, band=band)
        hi = sum(hits_in(h,s,e) for s,e in gts); ho=len(h)-hi
        din=hi/max(1e-6,rally_dur); do=ho/max(1e-6,dead_dur)
        ratio = din/do if do>0 else 99
        cov = sum(1 for s,e in gts if hits_in(h,s,e)>=2)
        bl = f"{band[0]}-{band[1]}" if band else "full"
        print(f"{bl:>14} {k:>4} {len(h)/dur*60:>8.1f} {din:>6.2f} {do:>7.2f} {ratio:>6.1f} {cov:>5}/{len(gts)}")
        if cov >= 0.8*len(gts) and (best is None or ratio>best[0]):
            best=(ratio,bl,k,cov)
print(f"\nBEST (cov>=80%): ratio {best[0]:.1f}x @ band={best[1]} k={best[2]} cov={best[3]}/{len(gts)}")
PY

### 973. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.996Z)

Important — and honest — **the band-pass didn't help.** Restricting to the HF "shuttle click" band gave *lower* discrimination (1.7–1.9×) than full-band, and the marginally-best was actually the *low* (voice) band (2.6× @ k=3.5). So on this GoPro audio, shuttle hits have **no spectral signature a band-pass can exploit** — the dead-time noise has similar transient energy across all bands. `k` (threshold) is the only real lever, and it trades coverage for discrimination (~3× costs half the rallies).

Before I conclude, let me test the leading suspect: are the silver labels' "dead time" regions actually full of *warm-up/knock-up* activity (which would unfairly depress the ratio)? And confirm the null result isn't Adarsh-specific.

### 974. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.996Z)

[external_agent_tool_call: Bash]
description: Warm-up/label-artifact check + TestLargeVideo confirm
command: cd "C:/Users/avidu/Projects/badminton-highlight-indexer"
python - <<'PY'
import os
from backend.audio.hit_detector import extract_audio, load_wav, detect_hits
from backend.eval.calibrate_wasb import load_golden_gts
from backend.utils.video import get_video_duration

def hits_in(h,s,e): return sum(1 for z in h if s<=z.t<=e)

def analyze(vid, labels, sr=32000, k=3.5, band=None):
    gts = sorted(load_golden_gts(labels)); dur = get_video_duration(vid)
    wav = extract_audio(vid, sr=sr); x, rsr = load_wav(wav); os.remove(wav)
    h = detect_hits(x, rsr, k=k, band=band)
    first_s = gts[0][0]; last_e = gts[-1][1]
    pre = hits_in(h, 0, first_s); pre_dur = max(1e-6, first_s)
    post = hits_in(h, last_e, dur); post_dur = max(1e-6, dur-last_e)
    in_r = sum(hits_in(h,s,e) for s,e in gts); rally_dur = sum(e-s for s,e in gts)
    # mid-match dead = gaps between consecutive rallies
    mid_dead_dur=0.0; mid_dead_hits=0
    for i in range(len(gts)-1):
        gs, ge = gts[i][1], gts[i+1][0]
        if ge>gs: mid_dead_dur += ge-gs; mid_dead_hits += hits_in(h, gs, ge)
    print(f"\n{os.path.basename(vid)} (k={k}, band={band}): {len(h)} hits, dur={dur:.0f}s, {len(gts)} rallies")
    print(f"  IN-RALLY      : {in_r/max(1e-6,rally_dur):.2f} hits/s  ({in_r} over {rally_dur:.0f}s)")
    print(f"  MID-MATCH DEAD: {mid_dead_hits/mid_dead_dur:.2f} hits/s  ({mid_dead_hits} over {mid_dead_dur:.0f}s)  <- the fair dead-time")
    print(f"  PRE-match     : {pre/pre_dur:.2f} hits/s  ({pre} over {pre_dur:.0f}s)  <- warm-up?")
    print(f"  POST-match    : {post/post_dur:.2f} hits/s  ({post} over {post_dur:.0f}s)")
    fair = (in_r/max(1e-6,rally_dur)) / max(1e-6, mid_dead_hits/max(1e-6,mid_dead_dur))
    print(f"  ==> FAIR discrimination (in-rally vs MID-MATCH dead): {fair:.1f}x")

analyze(r"C:/Users/avidu/Projects/Annotation Setup/Collect/Adarsh and Vasanth.MP4", "output/adarsh_gemini_fullvideo.csv")
analyze(r"C:/Users/avidu/Projects/Annotation Setup/Collect/TestLargeVideo.MP4", "output/testlarge_gemini_fullvideo.csv", k=3.5)
PY
[/external_agent_tool_call]

### 976. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.996Z)

The warm-up theory is **disproven** — the *mid-match* dead time (between rallies, the fair comparison) still runs **0.58–0.65 hits/s vs 1.27–1.48 in-rally → ~2.2× on both videos.** So the ceiling is real, not a label artifact: between-point activity (the loser tossing the shuttle back, footwork, talk) makes transients a classical onset detector can't tell from rally hits.

That's a clean, decisive Option-A result. Let me lock it in: add a band test, document the negative finding in the plan, run the suite, and commit to PR #35.

### 999. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:32.997Z)

Welcome back — hope the morning was good. 🙏 Here's the holistic look at Option A.

## Bottom line: classical-onset audio underdelivers on real GoPro audio — band-pass didn't rescue it

I ran the band-pass, a full **band × sensitivity sweep** (low/mid/high bands × k at 32 kHz), and a **label-artifact check**, on both Adarsh and TestLargeVideo. All committed to **PR #35** (209 tests green); both memory files refreshed.

**What the evidence says:**
- ✅ **Recall signal is real:** the detector fires in **100% of rallies** and **100% of WASB-missed rallies have hit-clusters** — audio genuinely lights up where play is.
- ❌ **Precision ceilings at ~2.2×** (in-rally vs dead-time hit density) on *both* videos — too low to be a clean cue (audio-only F1 0.13–0.27, below WASB).
- ❌ **Band-pass made it worse, not better.** The HF "shuttle-click" bands gave *lower* discrimination than full-band — shuttle hits have no band-separable signature in this audio.
- ❌ **Not a warm-up/label artifact.** The *mid-match* dead time (between rallies) alone runs ~0.6 hits/s vs ~1.3 in-rally → the 2.2× holds. **Root cause:** between-point activity — the loser tossing the shuttle back (literally the false-fire we're fighting), footwork, talk — makes transients a classical onset detector can't tell from rally hits.

**Honest reflection:** the research ranked audio #1, but on *your* GoPro audio it's weaker than the (broadcast/tennis) literature implied — exactly the shuttle-SNR + amateur-vs-broadcast caveat the research flagged. The good news is the **E1 gate did its job**: ~30 min of probing saved us from building the whole audio-fusion stack on a cue that wouldn't have carried it.

## Your call — where next

- **(A) Don't sink more into classical audio.** Get golden labels flowing (your VLC rating) to unblock the **distilled model + a real LOVO** on ≥3 videos, and let's hear your **"other hunch."** ← *my honest lean.*
- **(B) Try the one remaining audio lever: an E2 learned *timbre* classifier** (MFCC → shuttle-thwack vs toss/footstep/voice) — the only thing that can separate what a band-pass can't. But it **needs labeled hits** (real work; we'd bootstrap from your gold labels), so it's gated on data anyway.

### 1083. assistant (2026-07-02T06:11:33.001Z)

Everything's consolidated and the next incarnation is set up. Here's the sunset wrap.

## 🔊 Audio work — quantitative summary (gains & losses)

Recorded durably in **memory ([[audio-e1-findings]])** + **`docs/AUDIO_RALLY_DETECTION_PLAN.md` §10** (on master).

**GAINS**
| | Adarsh (36 rallies) | TestLargeVideo (7) |
|---|---|---|
| Rally coverage (≥2 hits) | 100% | 100% |
| Recall-recovery of WASB-missed rallies | 100% (14/14) | 100% (5/5) |

**LOSSES / LIMITS**
| | Adarsh | TestLargeVideo |
|---|---|---|
| Discrimination (in-rally ÷ dead-time) | ~2.2× | ~2.3× |
| Audio-only F1 (vs WASB-only) | 0.27 (vs 0.54) | 0.13 (vs 0.33) |
| Band-pass effect | none/negative | — |

**Verdict:** classical-onset audio is too weak to be a clean cue; band-pass can't fix it (it's a *timbre*, not a frequency-band, problem — between-point shuttle-tosses/footwork/talk fool the detector). Detector kept (merged, tested); **no fusion built** — the E1 gate paid for itself.

**The reusable lessons** (the real value, in [[audio-e1-findings]]): gate-before-build · test on the *real* distribution not the literature · discrimination-ratio is a fast cue screen (want ≫2×) · band ≠ timbre · silver labels for the gate / gold for the verdict · **golden labels are the recurring blocker across every cue**.

## 🧹 Repo state
master clean — **PRs #25–#35 merged**, 209 tests green, no open PRs, only `feat/khelacharya-shot-intelligence` retained.

**One honest flag:** PR **#36** (the *dedicated* `docs/AUDIO_E1_FINDINGS.md`) was **closed, not merged**, and I'd deleted its branch (the delete-before-confirming trap again — caught it). The doc isn't lost: commit `f643d64` is recoverable, and the full record is safe in memory + plan §10. If you want that dedicated repo doc, next session can re-land it in one step.

## 🧭 Resume plan (in current-state + handoff)
Next incarnation picks up on **either**:
1. **Golden evals** — you rate clips in the VLC annotator → `import-local`/`--labels` → unblocks the distilled-model LOVO (n≥3) + human-gold E1 re-confirm. *Highest-leverage.*
2. **Audio E2** — learned *timbre* classifier (needs labeled hits).

Also queued: pose/stance #2, your "other hunch", Option B (owned end-to-end model). Ramp-up kit (CODE_MAP, research, ADR-007, audio findings) is all linked.

**Memory fully refreshed** for sunset — current-state, session-handoff, the new audio-findings note, and the index all point a fresh session straight at the resume options.

This was a genuinely fun, productive run — WASB checkpointing, the distilled model, the Gemini teacher experiments, the rally-annotator release, and an honest audio go/no-go that saved us real build time. Thank you for the trust and the great instincts (the stance hunch, the local-first conviction, keeping me honest on the labels). Go rate some rallies — I'll be ready to dive into the audio classifier or the golden evals next time. 👋🌙
